After deriving the deviation laws for the invariant
manifold (Theorem
4
) and the conjugation (Theorem
5
),
we study the deviations of the dynamics from that of the averaged system (Theorem
6
).
The analysis is based on delicate correlation inequalities, which exploit the hyperbolicity
of
A_{0}
and make use of the map being weakly dissipative and the ensuing fact that any trajectory after
a while comes close enough to the attracting invariant manifold. A main issue –
and a major source of technical intricacies – is that the correlations
inequalities are related to the regularity of the involved functions. In
general, we have to deal with averages of the form
\langle g_{+}g_{-}{\circ}{\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{n}\rangle
,
with
g_{-}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{\alpha}^{-}(\Omega,\mathds{R})
, for which we can expect decay properties
analogous to those of Proposition
2.2
. However, the invariant
manifold and, hence, the map
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
are only
\alpha
-Hölder continuous, with

\alpha=O(\rho)
, so that a naiv̈e generalization of Proposition
2.2

would provide unavailing bounds. If we expand the average
\langle g_{+}g_{-}{\circ}{\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{n}\rangle
to second order in
W
, only the first order contributions
require a careful analysis, since the leading terms are regular and the second
order terms can be dealt with by using directly the bounds on the variance provided by
Theorem
4
. Instead, in order to use the bounds on the average in Theorem
4

we need to study in detail the first order contributions.
To this aim, we isolate the contributions which do not depend on the dynamics on the torus
and show that the remaining contributions admit better dimensional bounds.
To implement the scheme outlined above, we introduce a regularized
version
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
of the translated map, that we call the
auxiliary map
,
for which we can apply the correlation inequalities, and,
in Subsection
4.8.4
and Appendix
D
,
we compare the translated map with the auxiliary map through a series of technical lemmas
which aim at extracting and studying the linear dependence on the function
W
.
To do that, we need
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
to regularize
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
and, at the same time, still satisfy Hypotheses

1
to
3
: achieving both goals leads to contributions which,
albeit linear in
W
so that they be dealt with as outlined before,
unfortunately contain an extra factor
\rho^{-1}
. However such contributions
involve sums of terms in which there appear differences of functions
W
and the
sums can be rearranged in such a way that the difference is shifted to more
regular functions: this allows us to regain a further factor
\rho
so as to
compensate the factor
\rho^{-1}
. To implement the idea described above
we perform iterated expansions which make the analysis rather intricate:
in fact, Subsection
4.8.4
and Appendix
D
constitute the most
technical part of the paper.

In Section
5
, we prove Theorems
7
and
8

on the asymptotic behavior of the stochastic process associated to the
dynamics. Again, the crucial issue is that in average the deviations are small,
and hence the evolution of the system is essentially determined by the averaged map.

Appendix
A
, besides reviewing some basic facts about Anosov automorphisms,
contains the results on the decay of correlations for the
evolution generated by
A_{0}
on
\mathds{T}^{2}
. Such results form the main toolkit we
use in Section
4
to obtain more general correlation inequalities.
Appendices
B
,
C
and mainly – as said above –
D

contain mostly the proofs of the more technical results presented in Section
4
.

3
Mapping to a simpler model

In this section we prove Theorems
1
and
2
by explicitly solving (
2.12
) and (
2.14
).
The first one follows by relying on Banach fixed-point theorem,
while the second one exploits the dynamics being uniformly contracting around the invariant manifold.
Next, the two results together are showed to yield Theorem
3
.

3.1
The invariant manifold: proof of Theorem
1

From (
2.12
) we get

W(A_{0}\psi)=G(W(\psi),\psi)=W(\psi)+F(W(\psi),\psi).

(3.1)

To show that a solution of (
3.1
) exists we define the map

{\mathscr{E}}[W](\psi):=G(W(A_{0}^{-1}\psi),A_{0}^{-1}\psi),

(3.2)

so that the invariant manifold is the solution of the fixed point
equation
{\mathscr{E}}[W]=W
.
If we define the set

B:=\{W\in{\mathfrak{B}}(\mathds{T}^{2},\mathds{R}):S_{m}\leq W\leq S_{M}\}
,
then
{\mathscr{E}}(B)\subset B
by Hypotheses
1
and
2
. Moreover

\|{\mathscr{E}}[W_{1}]-{\mathscr{E}}[W_{2}]\|_{\infty}=\sup_{\psi\in\mathds{T}^{2}}\left|G(W_{1}(A_{0}^{-1}\psi),A_{0}^{-1}\psi)-G(W_{2}(A_{0}^{-1}\psi),A_{0}^{-1}\psi)\right|\leq(1-\Gamma)\|W_{1}-W_{2}\|_{\infty},

that is
{\mathscr{E}}
is a contraction on
B
, and thus, by the Banach fixed-point theorem, there is a unique
W\in B
that satisfies (
3.1
).
We pass to discuss the regularity of
W
. Since
F
is
\alpha_{0}
-Hölder continuous, we get

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{E}}[W]|_{\alpha_{+}}^{+}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{-\alpha_{+}}\sup_{\psi\in\mathds{T}^{2}}\sup_{x\in\mathds{R}}|x|^{-\alpha_{+}}\Big(|G(W(\psi+xv_{+}),\psi+xv_{+})-G(W(\psi),\psi+xv_{+})|

(3.3)

\displaystyle+|G(W(\psi),\psi+xv_{+})-G(W(\psi),\psi)|\Bigr)\leq\lambda^{-\alpha_{+}}\bigl((1-\Gamma)|W|_{\alpha_{+}}^{+}+|F|_{\alpha_{+}}\bigr),

for
\alpha_{+}\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
, and similarly we obtain, for
\alpha_{-}\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
,

|{\mathscr{E}}[W]|_{\alpha_{-}}^{-}\leq\lambda^{\alpha_{-}}\bigl((1-\Gamma)|W|_{\alpha_{-}}^{-}+|F|_{\alpha_{-}}\bigr).

(3.4)

This implies that the set

B_{\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}}:=\{W\in{\mathfrak{B}}_{\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}}(\mathds{T}^{2},\mathds{R}):|W|_{\alpha_{+}}^{+}\leq\delta_{+}|F|_{\alpha_{+}}\hbox{ and }|W|_{\alpha_{-}}^{+}\leq\delta_{-}|F|_{\alpha_{-}}\}
,
with
\delta_{\pm}:=(\lambda^{\pm\alpha_{\pm}}\!-\!(1\!-\!\Gamma))^{-1}
,
is invariant under
{\mathscr{E}}
.
Thus, we need to show that
{\mathscr{E}}
is a contraction on
B_{\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}}
for suitable
\alpha_{-}
and
\alpha_{+}
,
in order to apply once more the Banach Fixed-Point Theorem. To this end,
observe that, for any
W_{1},W_{2}\in B_{\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}}
we bound

|{\mathscr{E}}[W_{1}]-{\mathscr{E}}[W_{2}]|_{\alpha_{+}}^{+}
by

\displaystyle\lambda^{-\alpha_{+}}\sup_{\psi\in\mathds{T}^{2}}\sup_{x\in\mathds{R}}|x|^{-\alpha_{+}}\Bigl(\,\Bigl|G(W_{1}(\psi),\psi)-G(W_{2}(\psi),\psi)-G(W_{1}(\psi+xv_{+}),\psi)+G(W_{2}(\psi+xv_{+}),\psi)\Bigr|\phantom{\sup_{x}}

\displaystyle+\Bigl|G(W_{1}(\psi\!+\!xv_{+}),\psi)-G(W_{2}(\psi\!+\!xv_{+}),\psi)-G(W_{1}(\psi\!+\!xv_{+}),\psi\!+\!xv_{+})+G(W_{2}(\psi\!+\!xv_{+}),\psi\!+\!xv_{+})\Bigr|\,\Bigr),

where, using twice a standard interpolation between
G(W_{1}(\psi_{1}),\psi_{2})
and

G(W_{2}(\psi_{1}),\psi_{2})
in the second line and a 4 point interpolation in the first line we get

|{\mathscr{E}}[W_{1}]\!-\!{\mathscr{E}}[W_{2}]|_{\alpha_{+}}^{+}\!\leq\!\lambda^{-\alpha_{+}}\Bigl((1\!-\!\Gamma)|W_{1}\!-\!W_{2}|_{\alpha_{+}}^{+}\!+\!\bigl(\|\partial_{\varphi}^{2}F\|_{\infty}\max_{i=1,2}|W_{i}|_{\alpha_{+}}^{+}\!+\!|\partial_{\varphi}F|_{\alpha_{+}}^{+}\bigr)\|W_{1}\!-\!W_{2}\|_{\infty}\Bigr).

(3.5)

Analogously, we obtain

|{\mathscr{E}}[W_{1}]\!-\!{\mathscr{E}}[W_{2}]|_{\alpha_{-}}^{-}\!\leq\!\lambda^{\alpha_{-}}\Bigl((1\!-\!\Gamma)|W_{1}\!-\!W_{2}|_{\alpha_{-}}^{-}\!+\!\bigl(\|\partial_{\varphi}^{2}F\|_{\infty}\max_{i=1,2}|W_{i}|_{\alpha_{-}}^{-}\!+\!|\partial_{\varphi}F|_{\alpha_{-}}^{-}\bigr)\|W_{1}\!-\!W_{2}\|_{\infty}\Bigr).

(3.6)

Let now fix
\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
so as to satisfy
\lambda^{\alpha_{-}}(1-\Gamma)<1
and

\alpha_{+}\lambda^{-\alpha_{+}}\!\biggl(\frac{\|\partial_{\varphi}^{2}F\|_{\infty}|F|_{\alpha_{+}}}{\lambda^{\alpha_{+}}-(1\!-\!\Gamma)}+|\partial_{\varphi}F|_{\alpha_{+}}^{+}\biggr)+\alpha_{-}\lambda^{\alpha_{-}}\!\biggl(\frac{\lambda^{\alpha_{-}}\|\partial_{\varphi}^{2}F\|_{\infty}|F|_{\alpha_{-}}}{1-\lambda^{\alpha_{-}}(1\!-\!\Gamma)}+|\partial_{\varphi}F|_{\alpha_{-}}^{-}\biggr)\!<\Gamma.

(3.7)

For such
\alpha_{-}
and
\alpha_{+}
, combining the bounds (
3.3
), (
3.4
), (
3.5
) and (
3.6
),
the map
{\mathscr{E}}
turns out to be a contraction on
B_{\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}}
.
This concludes the proof of Theorem
1
.

Recalling that
S_{m}<0<S_{M}
, by Remark
2.6
, the discussion above implies that the sequence
{\mathscr{E}}^{n}[0]
converges
to
W
in
{\mathfrak{B}}(\mathds{T}^{2},\mathds{R})
. Moreover the inequalities above are satisfied for
\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}

small enough; in particular, if
\rho
is small and
|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|F|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{\alpha_{0},2}=O(\rho)
, then the condition
\lambda^{\alpha_{-}}(1-\Gamma)<1

requires
\alpha_{-}=O(\rho)
, which inserted into (
3.7
) allows
\alpha_{+}
to be
O(1)
in
\rho
.

In the scaling regime, where
F
and its derivatives are proportional to
\rho
,
the best we can say about the invariant manifold is that

W\in B\cap B_{\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}}

and hence
|W|\leq\max\{|S_{m}|,S_{M}\}
,
\alpha_{+}=O(1)
and
\alpha_{-}=O(\rho)
,
so that we have
\|W\|_{\infty}=O(1)
,

|W|_{\alpha_{-}}^{-}=O(1)
and
|W|_{\alpha_{+}}^{+}=O(\rho)
.
This proves Corollary
2.14
.

Remark 3.1
.

If
\psi_{0}
is a fixed point of
A_{0}
, that is
A_{0}\psi_{0}=\psi_{0}
, from (
3.1
) we get

W(\psi_{0})=S(\psi_{0})
, so that, in the scaling regime,
W(\psi_{0})
does not depend on
\rho
.
In a similar way, if
\psi_{k}
is a periodic point of period
k
, that is
A_{0}^{k}\psi_{k}=\psi_{k}
,
then
W(\psi_{k})
is found between
\min_{i}S(A_{0}^{i}\psi_{k})
and
\max_{i}S(A_{0}^{i}\psi_{k})
. This
shows that, in the scaling regime, in general
\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}W(\psi)\not=0
.

3.2
The conjugation: proof of Theorem
2

3.2.1
The translated map

We construct the conjugation
{\mathscr{H}}
by
linearizing the dynamics around the invariant manifold.
We write

\varphi=\theta+W(\psi)
so that, in terms of the variables
(\theta,\psi)
, the
dynamics is described by the map

{\mathscr{S}}_{1}(\theta,\psi)=(({\mathscr{S}}_{1})_{\theta}(\theta,\psi),({\mathscr{S}}_{1})_{\psi}(\theta,\psi)):=(G_{1}(\theta,\psi),A_{0}\psi),\qquad G_{1}(\theta,\psi):=\theta+F_{1}(\theta,\psi),

(3.8)

with

F_{1}(\theta,\psi):=F(\theta+W(\psi),\psi)-F(W(\psi),\psi)=F(\theta+W(\psi),\psi)+W(\psi)-W(A_{0}\psi).

(3.9)

We call
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
the
translated map
. Defining

\Omega_{1}:=\{(\theta,\psi)\in\mathds{T}\times\mathds{T}^{2}\,:\,\phi_{m}-W(\psi)\leq\theta\leq\phi_{M}-W(\psi)\}
,
we have that
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
is injective from
\Omega_{1}
into itself.

Remark 3.2
.

One easily checks that
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}(0,\psi)=(0,A_{0}\psi)
, so that, expressed in terms
of
\theta
, the invariant manifold reduces to

\overline{{\mathcal{W}}}=\{(0,\psi):\psi\in\mathds{T}^{2}\}
(see Remark
2.17
).

Remark 3.3
.

The iterations of
{\mathscr{S}}
and
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
are such that

({\mathscr{S}}^{n})_{\varphi}(\varphi,\psi)=({\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{n})_{\theta}(\varphi-W(\psi),\psi)+W(A_{0}^{n}\psi)

and

({\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{n})_{\theta}(\theta,\psi)=({\mathscr{S}}^{n})_{\varphi}(\theta+W(\psi),\psi)-W(A_{0}^{n}\psi)
,
while
({\mathscr{S}}^{n})_{\psi}(\varphi,\psi)=({\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{n})_{\psi}(\varphi-W(\psi),\psi)=A_{0}^{n}\psi
.

In Subsections
3.2.2
and
3.2.3
we study the conjugation relation

{\mathscr{H}}_{1}{\circ}{\mathscr{S}}_{1}={\mathscr{S}}_{0}{\circ}{\mathscr{H}}_{1}.

(3.10)

where
{\mathscr{H}}_{1}:\Omega_{1}\to\Omega_{0}
is of the form

{\mathscr{H}}_{1}(\theta,\psi)=({\mathcal{H}}_{1}(\theta,\psi),\psi)
. We can then write

{\mathscr{H}}(\varphi,\psi)=({\mathcal{H}}_{1}(\varphi-W(\psi),\psi),\psi),\vskip-2.84544pt

(3.11)

with
{\mathscr{H}}
satisfying the conjugation relation (
2.14
). Thus, if the conjugation
{\mathscr{H}}_{1}
exists and is invertible,
the conjugation
{\mathscr{H}}
exists and is invertible as well, and vice versa.
Moreover,
{\mathscr{H}}
and
{\mathscr{H}}_{1}
have the same image
\Omega_{0}
defined in Theorem
2
.
In analogy with (
3.11
), we write
{\mathscr{H}}_{1}^{-1}(\eta,\psi)=({\mathcal{L}}_{1}(\eta,\psi),\psi)
.

Considering also Remark
2.10
,
we look for functions
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}\!:\Omega_{1}\to\mathds{R}
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}:\Omega_{0}\to\mathds{R}
of the form

{\mathcal{H}}_{1}(\theta,\psi)=\theta+\theta^{2}h_{1}(\theta,\psi),\qquad{\mathcal{L}}_{1}(\eta,\psi)=\eta+\eta^{2}l_{1}(\eta,\psi).\vskip-2.84544pt

(3.12)

If we show that functions of the form (
3.12
) exist,
with
h_{1}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{\alpha_{*},1}(\Omega_{1},\mathds{R})
and
l_{1}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{\alpha_{*},1}(\Omega_{0},\mathds{R})
,
for a suitable
\alpha_{*}\in(0,\alpha_{0})
, then we can write the conjugation
{\mathscr{H}}
and its inverse as in (
2.15
),
with

h(\varphi,\psi)=h_{1}(\varphi-W(\psi),\psi),\qquad l(\eta,\psi)=l_{1}(\eta,\psi).\vskip-2.84544pt

(3.13)

Remark 3.4
.

As a consequence of Theorem
1
, there exists a closed interval

\Theta:=[\theta_{-},\theta_{+}]
,
such that
\Theta\times\mathds{T}^{2}\subset\Omega_{1}
and hence

\{(\varphi,\psi)\in\mathds{T}\times\mathds{T}^{2}\,:\,\varphi-W(\psi)\in\Theta\}\subset\Omega
.

3.2.2
Dynamics toward the invariant manifold

From (
3.10
) we see that
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
satisfies the equation

\kappa(\psi){\mathcal{H}}_{1}(\theta,\psi)={\mathcal{H}}_{1}({\mathscr{S}}_{1}(\theta,\psi))
.
Using (
3.12
), we find

h_{1}(\theta,\psi)=(\kappa(\psi)\theta^{2})^{-1}\bigl(G_{1}(\theta,\psi)+G_{1}(\theta,\psi)^{2}h_{1}({\mathscr{S}}_{1}(\theta,\psi))-\kappa(\psi)\,\theta\bigr)
,
so that, setting

\displaystyle p_{1}(\theta,\psi):=

\displaystyle\frac{(G_{1}(\theta,\psi))^{2}}{\theta^{2}\kappa(\psi)}=\frac{1}{\kappa(\psi)}\left(1+\int_{0}^{1}{\rm d}t\,\partial_{\varphi}F(t\theta+W(\psi),\psi)\right)^{2},

(3.14a)

\displaystyle q_{1}(\theta,\psi):=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{1}(\theta,\psi)-\theta\kappa(\psi)}{\theta^{2}\kappa(\psi)}=\frac{1}{\kappa(\psi)}\int_{0}^{1}{\rm d}t\,(1-t)\,\partial_{\varphi}^{2}F(t\theta+W(\psi),\psi)\,,

(3.14b)

we obtain

h_{1}(\theta,\psi)=q_{1}(\theta,\psi)+p_{1}(\theta,\psi)\,h_{1}({\mathscr{S}}_{1}(\theta,\psi))
,
whose solution can be formally written
as

h_{1}(\theta,\psi)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}p_{1}^{(n)}(\theta,\psi)\,q_{1}({\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{n}(\theta,\psi)),\qquad p^{(n)}_{1}(\theta,\psi):=\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}p_{1}({\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{i}(\theta,\psi)).\vskip-5.69046pt

(3.15)

where, according to our conventions (see Remark
2.24
),we have
p^{(0)}_{1}(\theta,\psi)=1
.

Remark 3.5
.

Both
p_{1}(\theta,\psi)
and
q_{1}(\theta,\psi)
are well defined
at
\theta=0
. Using (
3.1
) and the definition
of
\kappa(\psi)
after (
2.13
), we find
\kappa(\psi)\,q_{1}(0,\psi)=\partial_{\varphi}^{2}F(0,\psi)
and
p_{1}(0,\psi)=\kappa(\psi)
.

We start by studying the regularity properties of the functions
p_{1}
and
q_{1}
. The
following result is an easy consequence of the representation in (
3.14
)
together with Lemma
2.5
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Assume
{\mathscr{S}}
in (
2.9
) to satisfy Hypotheses

1
–
3
.
For any
\alpha\in(0,\min\{\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}\})
, with
\alpha_{-}
and
\alpha_{+}
as in Theorem
1
,
and any
\Gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\Gamma)
,
one has
p_{1},q_{1}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{\alpha,5}(\Omega_{1},\mathds{R})
and

p_{1}(\theta,\psi)\leq 1-\Gamma^{\prime}

as long as
|\theta|\leq\theta_{1}
, with

\theta_{1}:=\frac{\Gamma-\Gamma^{\prime}}{2\|\kappa^{-1}\|_{\infty}(1+\|\partial_{\varphi}F\|_{\infty})\|\partial_{\varphi}^{2}F\|_{\infty}}.\vskip 2.84544pt

(3.16)

Proof.

Observe that
p_{1}(0,\psi)=1+\partial_{\varphi}F(W(\psi),\psi)\leq 1-\Gamma
. Thus,
using that, for any
\theta_{1}>0
and all
\theta\in[-\theta_{1},\theta_{1}]
, we have

|p_{1}(\theta,\psi)-p_{1}(0,\psi)|\leq\|\partial_{\theta}p_{1}\|_{\infty}\theta_{1}
,
if we bound
\partial_{\theta}p_{1}
using (
3.14a
) and fix
\theta_{1}
as in
(
3.16
), the bound on
p_{1}(\theta,\psi)
for
|\theta|\leq\theta_{1}

follows immediately. The other bounds too are easily obtained by estimating the
derivatives of
p_{1}
and
q_{1}
.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

In the scaling regime, where
\Gamma=\rho\,\gamma
, one has

|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|p_{1}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{\alpha,5}=1+O(\rho)
and

|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|q_{1}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{\alpha,5}=O(\rho)
,
while
|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|\partial_{\theta}p_{1}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,4}=O(\rho)
and
|p_{1}|_{\alpha}=O(\rho)
.
Moreover, if in Lemma
3.6
one takes
\Gamma^{\prime}=\rho\,\gamma^{\prime}

with
\gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\gamma)
, then (
3.16
) implies that
\theta_{1}=O(1)
.

Next, we study the regularity of the iterates of
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
.

Lemma 3.8
.

Assume
{\mathscr{S}}
in (
2.9
) to satisfy Hypotheses

1
–
3
.
For any
\alpha

\in(0,\min\{\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}\})
, with
\alpha_{-}
and
\alpha_{+}
as in Theorem
1
,
and any
\Gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\Gamma)
, and all
n\geq 0
, one has
({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}\!\in\!{\mathscr{B}}_{\alpha,2}(\Omega_{1},\mathds{R})
and

|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|\partial_{\theta}({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,1}\leq D_{0}\left(1-\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{n},\qquad|({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}|_{\alpha}\leq D_{0}\lambda^{\alpha n},\qquad|\partial_{\theta}({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}|_{\alpha}\leq D_{0}\lambda^{\alpha n},

with the constant
D_{0}
depending on
F
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
but not on
n
.

Proof.

For a fixed
\Gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\Gamma)
, let
r=r(\Gamma^{\prime})
and
N_{r}
be defined as in Lemma

2.5
, and observe that
N_{r}=O((\Gamma^{\prime})^{-1})
in such a case. We have

\|\partial_{\theta}G_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1+\|\partial_{\varphi}F\|_{\infty}
, while
for
n\geq N_{r}
we may bound

\|(\partial_{\theta}G_{1}){\circ}{\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq\|(1+\partial_{\varphi}F){\circ}{\mathscr{S}}^{n}\|_{\infty}\leq(1-\Gamma^{\prime})
.
Thus, noting that
({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}=G_{1}{\circ}{\mathscr{S}}^{n-1}_{1}
, we get

\|\partial_{\theta}({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}\|_{\infty}\leq\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}\|(\partial_{\theta}G_{1}){\circ}{\mathscr{S}}^{i}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq c_{1}\left(1-\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{n},\vskip-2.84544pt

(3.17)

with
c_{1}\!:=\!(\,(1\!+\!\|\partial_{\varphi}F\|_{\infty})/(1\!-\!\Gamma^{\prime}))^{N_{r}}
,
and
|G_{1}{\circ}{\mathscr{S}}^{n-1}_{1}|_{\alpha}\!\leq\!\|(\partial_{\theta}G_{1}){\circ}{\mathscr{S}}^{n-1}_{1}\|_{\infty}|G_{1}{\circ}{\mathscr{S}}^{n-2}_{1}|_{\alpha}\!+\!\lambda^{\alpha(n-1)}|G_{1}|_{\alpha}
,
where
|G_{1}|_{\alpha}\leq\|\partial_{\varphi}F\|_{\infty}|W|_{\alpha}+|F|_{\alpha}
,
and hence, iterating, we get

\hbox{}\hskip-5.69046pt|({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}|_{\alpha}\leq|G_{1}|_{\alpha}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda^{\alpha(n-i)}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\|(\partial_{\theta}G_{1}){\circ}{\mathscr{S}}^{n-j}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}|G_{1}|_{\alpha}\,\lambda^{\alpha n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1\!-\!\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{i-1}\lambda^{-\alpha i}\leq c_{2}\lambda^{\alpha n},\vskip-2.84544pt

(3.18)

with
c_{2}:=c_{1}|G_{1}|_{\alpha}\,(\Gamma^{\prime})^{-1}
.
Exploiting (
3.17
), we find that

\|\partial_{\theta}^{2}({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left\|(\partial_{\theta}^{2}G_{1}){\circ}{\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\partial_{\theta}({\mathscr{S}}^{i}_{1})_{\theta}\right\|_{\infty}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=0\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{n-1}\|(\partial_{\theta}G_{1}){\circ}{\mathscr{S}}^{j}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq c_{3}\left(1-\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{n},\vskip-5.69046pt

(3.19)

with

c_{3}:=c_{1}\|\partial_{\varphi}^{2}F\|_{\infty}\,(\Gamma^{\prime})^{-1}
.
Finally, noting that

\left|\partial_{\theta}({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}\right|_{\alpha}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\Bigl(\left\|\partial_{\theta}^{2}G_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\left|({\mathscr{S}}^{i}_{1})_{\theta}\right|_{\alpha}+\lambda^{\alpha i}|\partial_{\theta}G_{1}|_{\alpha}\Bigr)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{n-1}\|(\partial_{\theta}G_{1}){\circ}{\mathscr{S}}^{j}_{1}\|_{\infty},\vskip-5.69046pt

and proceeding as done to get the bound (
3.18
), we obtain

|\partial_{\theta}({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}|_{\alpha}\leq c_{4}\lambda^{\alpha n},\vskip-2.84544pt

(3.20)

with

c_{4}:=\bigl(c_{2}\|\partial_{\varphi}^{2}F\|_{\infty}+|\partial_{\theta}G_{1}|_{\alpha}\bigr)\,(\Gamma^{\prime})^{-1}
.
Collecting together the bounds (
3.17
)–(
3.20
), the assertion follows
follow with
D_{0}=\max\{c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4}\}
.
∎

Remark 3.9
.

In the scaling regime, where
\Gamma=\rho\,\gamma
, if we choose
\Gamma^{\prime}=\rho\gamma^{\prime}
, with
\gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\gamma)
,
we find that the constant
D_{0}
is
O(1)
in
\rho
. Indeed both
|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|F|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,2}
and
|F|_{\alpha}
, and hence
|G_{1}|_{\alpha}
as well,
are
O(\rho)
, while one has
N_{r}=O(\rho^{-1})
by (
2.11
).

Remark 3.10
.

Since
({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}(0,\psi)=0
for all
n\in\mathds{Z}
, we bound also

|({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}(\theta,\psi)|\leq\|\partial_{\theta}({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}\|_{\infty}|\theta|
and hence

\|({\mathscr{S}}^{n}_{1})_{\theta}\|_{\infty}\leq c_{1}(\max\{|\phi_{m}|,\phi_{M}\}+\max\{|S_{m}|,S_{M}\})(1-\Gamma^{\prime})^{n}
.

3.2.3
Existence and regularity of the conjugation

We have all the ingredients to complete the proof of Theorem
2
.
Fix
\Gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\Gamma)
and set
r=r(\Gamma^{\prime})
,

S_{r}:=\max\{S_{M},|S_{m}|\}+r
and
M_{r}:=\max\{2\,(\Gamma^{\prime})^{-1}\log(S_{r}/\theta_{1}),0\}
,
with the notation of Lemma
2.5
and
\theta_{1}
as in (
3.16
).
If
(\theta+W(\psi),\psi)\in\Lambda_{r}
and
n>M_{r}
, then

|({\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{n}(\theta,\psi))_{\theta}|\leq\theta_{1}
and hence, thanks to the property
3
in Lemma
2.5
,

|({\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{n}(\theta,\psi))_{\theta}|\leq\theta_{1}
for all
(\theta,\psi)\in\Omega_{1}
and for
all
n\geq M_{r}^{\prime}:=M_{r}+N_{r}
. Thus, for
n\geq M_{r}^{\prime}
, we obtain

\|p_{1}^{(n)}\|_{\infty}\leq\|p_{1}\|_{\infty}^{M_{r}^{\prime}}(1-\Gamma^{\prime})^{n-M_{r}^{\prime}}

which, inserted in (
3.15
), implies

\|h_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq D_{1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(1-\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{n}\|q_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq D_{1}(\Gamma^{\prime})^{-1}\|q_{1}\|_{\infty},\qquad\qquad D_{1}:=\left(\frac{\|p_{1}\|_{\infty}}{1-\Gamma^{\prime}}\right)^{M_{r}^{\prime}}.\vskip-2.84544pt

(3.21)

Furthermore, using Lemma
3.8
, we see that

\bigl\|\partial_{\theta}p_{1}^{(n)}\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq D_{0}D_{1}\|\partial_{\theta}p_{1}\|_{\infty}\left(1-\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{n-1}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left(1-\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{i}\leq D_{0}D_{1}(\Gamma^{\prime})^{-1}\|\partial_{\theta}p_{1}\|_{\infty}\left(1-\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{n}\,,\vskip-2.84544pt

(3.22)

with
D_{0}
as in Lemma
3.8
and
D_{1}
as in (
3.21
), so that, summing over
n
, we get

\|\partial_{\theta}h_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(\bigl\|\partial_{\theta}p_{1}^{(n)}\bigr\|_{\infty}\|q_{1}\|_{\infty}+\bigl\|p_{1}^{(n)}\bigr\|_{\infty}\|\partial_{\theta}(q_{1}{\circ}{\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{n})\|_{\infty}\right)\leq D_{2}\,(\Gamma^{\prime})^{-1}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|q|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,1}\,,\vskip-2.84544pt

(3.23)

for some constant
D_{2}
.
Proceeding along the same lines, we obtain also (see Appendix
B.1
for details)

\|\partial_{\theta}^{2}h_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq D_{3}\,(\Gamma^{\prime})^{-1}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|q|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,2}\,,

(3.24)

for some other constant
D_{3}
.
Finally, for any
\alpha\in(0,\min\{\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}\}]
, we get, again relying on Lemma
3.8
,

\bigl|p^{(n)}_{1}\bigr|_{\alpha}\leq D_{0}D_{1}(|p_{1}|_{\alpha}+\|\partial_{\theta}p_{1}\|_{\infty})\left(1-\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{n-1}\frac{\lambda^{\alpha n}-1}{\lambda^{\alpha}-1}\leq D_{4}\,\lambda^{\alpha n}\left(1-\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{n}\,,

for a suitable constant
D_{4}
proportional to
D_{1}
.
To sum over
n
we take
\alpha=\alpha_{*}
in Lemma
3.8
, with
\alpha_{*}
such that

\lambda^{\alpha_{*}}(1-\Gamma^{\prime})<1-\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}<\Gamma^{\prime}
, so as to obtain

|h_{1}|_{\alpha_{*}}\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(\bigl|p_{1}^{(n)}\bigr|_{\alpha_{*}}\|q\|_{\infty}+\bigl\|p_{1}^{(n)}\bigr\|_{\infty}|q_{1}{\circ}{\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{n}|_{\alpha_{*}}\right)\leq D_{4}\,(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})^{-1}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|q_{1}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{\alpha_{*},1},\vskip-2.84544pt

(3.25)

for a suitable constant
D_{4}
proportional to
D_{1}
.
Once more we bound
|\partial_{\theta}h_{1}|_{\alpha_{*}}

by reasoning in a similar way (again we refer to Appendix
B.1
for details) and find

|\partial_{\theta}h_{1}|_{\alpha_{*}}\leq D_{5}\,(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})^{-1}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|q_{1}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{\alpha_{*},1},

(3.26)

with
D_{5}
proportional to
D_{2}
.
The bounds (
3.21
) and (
3.23
)–(
3.26
)
ensure that
\|h_{1}\|_{\alpha_{*},2}
is bounded.
Therefore, recalling the first relation in (
3.13
), Theorem
2

is proved, with
\Omega_{0}={\mathscr{H}}_{1}(\Omega_{1})={\mathscr{H}}(\Omega)
.

Remark 3.11
.

The argument above shows that it is enough to prove the existence of the conjugation
inside
\Lambda
.
Indeed, once the conjugation has been defined in
\Lambda
, it can be extended to the whole
\Omega
by using
that all trajectories fall inside a neighborhood of the attracting
invariant manifold in a finite time.

With the notation of Lemma
2.5
, for
r=r(\Gamma^{\prime})
we have
N_{r}=O((\Gamma^{\prime})^{-1})
.
Thus, from Remark
3.9
it is easy to see that in the scaling regime
the constant
D_{0}
in Lemma
3.8
is
O(1)
in
\rho
.
We can take
\Gamma^{\prime}=\rho\gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\rho\gamma^{\prime\prime}
, with
0<\gamma^{\prime\prime}<\gamma^{\prime}<\gamma
.
Using that
\theta_{1}=O(1)
in
\rho
(see Remark
3.7
),
so that
M_{r}=O(\rho^{-1})
and hence
M_{r}^{\prime}=O((\Gamma^{\prime})^{-1})
as well, and requiring

\alpha_{*}
in Theorem
2
to be such that

\lambda^{\alpha_{*}}(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})<(1-\rho\gamma^{\prime\prime})
, so that
\alpha_{*}=O(\rho)
,
we easily check that
D_{1}
in (
3.21
) and, as a consequence,
the constants
D_{2}
,
D_{3}
and
D_{4}
as well are all
O(1)
in
\rho
.
This implies that both
|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|h|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,1}
and
|h|_{\alpha_{*}}
are
O(1)
in
\rho
.
Collecting together the bounds above immediately implies Corollary
2.15
.

3.2.4
The inverse conjugation

Since
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}(\theta,\psi)=\theta+\theta^{2}h_{1}(\theta,\psi)
and
h_{1}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{\alpha_{*},1}({\Omega_{1}},\mathds{R})
for a suitable
\alpha_{*}>0
,
there exists
\theta_{*}\leq\theta_{1}
such that

\partial_{\theta}{\mathcal{H}}_{1}(\theta,\psi)>1/2
for
|\theta|<\theta_{*}
.
We have

{\mathcal{H}}_{1}(\theta,\psi)=(\kappa^{(n)}(\psi))^{-1}{\mathcal{H}}_{1}({\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{n}(\theta,\psi))

for all
(\theta,\psi)\in\Omega_{1}
and all
n\in\mathds{N}
.
If we reason like deriving the bound on
\|p_{1}^{(n)}\|_{\infty}
in Subsection
3.2.3

we see that there exists

M_{2}
such that
|({\mathscr{S}}_{1}^{M_{2}}(\theta,\psi))_{\theta}|\leq\theta_{*}
. Thus,
by Lemma
2.5
and Hypothesis
1
,
we get, for all
(\theta,\psi)\in\Omega_{1}
,

\partial_{\theta}{\mathcal{H}}_{1}(\theta,\psi)\geq\|\kappa\|_{\infty}^{-M_{2}}\inf_{|\theta|\leq\theta^{\star}}\partial_{\theta}{\mathcal{H}}_{1}(\theta,\psi)\left(\inf_{(\theta,\psi)\in\Omega_{1}}\partial_{\theta}G_{1}(\theta,\psi)\right)^{M_{2}}=:\tau_{1}>0.\vskip-2.84544pt

(3.27)

By the inverse function theorem, there is

l_{1}\!\in\!{\mathscr{B}}_{0,1}({\mathscr{H}}_{1}(\Omega_{1},\mathds{R}))
such that

({\mathscr{H}}_{1}^{-1})_{\eta}(\eta,\psi)\!=\!\eta+\eta^{2}l_{1}(\eta,\psi)
.
Furthermore, for any
\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in\mathds{T}^{2}
, we can write

{\mathcal{H}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{-1}(\eta,\psi^{\prime}),\psi^{\prime})-{\mathcal{H}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{-1}(\eta,\psi),\psi)=0
,
so that

|{\mathcal{H}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{-1}(\eta,\psi^{\prime}),\psi^{\prime})-{\mathcal{H}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{-1}(\eta,\psi),\psi^{\prime})|=|{\mathcal{H}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{-1}(\eta,\psi),\psi^{\prime})-{\mathcal{H}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{-1}(\eta,\psi),\psi)|
,
which, divided by
|\psi^{\prime}-\psi|^{\alpha_{*}}
, gives

\partial_{\theta}{\mathcal{H}}_{1}(\theta,\psi)\,|{\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{-1}|_{\alpha_{*}}\leq|{\mathcal{H}}_{1}|_{\alpha_{*}}
for all
(\theta,\psi)\in\Omega_{1}
,
so that we obtain
|{\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{-1}|_{\alpha_{*}}\leq\tau_{1}^{-1}|{\mathcal{H}}_{1}|_{\alpha_{*}}
.
Therefore, also
l_{1}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{\alpha_{*},1}({\mathscr{H}}_{1}(\Omega_{1}),\mathds{R})

and, by the second relation of (
3.13
), Corollorary
2.12
follows.

Remark 3.12
.

By reasoning as in the derivation of (
3.27
), we find that there exists
\bar{\tau}>0
such that

\partial_{\theta}\overline{{\mathcal{H}}}(\theta)\geq\bar{\tau}
for all
\theta\in{\mathcal{U}}
. This will be used later on (see Subsection
4.9
).

3.3
Physical measure: proof of Theorem
3

Let
{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{2}
be two observables in
{\mathscr{B}}_{\alpha_{*},1}(\Omega,\mathds{R})
.
By Remark
3.10
, we have

|{\mathcal{O}}_{2}({\mathscr{S}}^{n}(\varphi,\psi))-{\mathcal{O}}_{2}(W(A_{0}^{n}\psi),A_{0}^{n}\psi)|\leq C(1-\Gamma^{\prime})^{n}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\mathcal{O}}_{2}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,1},\vskip-2.84544pt

for a suitable constant
C
.
Thus, if
\nu_{W}
and
\nu_{0}
are defined as in Subsection
2.3.3
, we find

\displaystyle\left|\nu_{0}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{1}\,{\mathcal{O}}_{2}{\circ}{\mathscr{S}}^{n}\right)-\nu_{0}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{1}\right)\nu_{W}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int d\varphi\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{1}(\varphi,\cdot){\mathcal{O}}_{2}(W(A_{0}^{n}\cdot),A_{0}^{n}\cdot)\right\rangle-\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{1}(\varphi,\cdot)\rangle\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{2}(W(\cdot),\cdot)\rangle\right|+C(1-\Gamma^{\prime})^{n}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\mathcal{O}}_{2}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,1}\|{\mathcal{O}}_{1}\|_{\infty}.

Since
W\in{\mathfrak{B}}_{\alpha_{-}}^{-}(\mathds{T}^{2},\mathds{R})
,
relying on Remark
2.3
, we obtain the bound in Theorem
3
.

4
Averaging and deviations

The study of the convergence of the dynamics of
{\mathscr{S}}
to the deterministic dynamics
\Phi_{n\rho}
is
structured in several steps: we start with the first and second moments of

W
(Subsections
4.2
and
4.3
);
then we consider the moments of the functions
h
and
l
and their derivatives (Subsections
4.4
to
4.9
);
eventually we draw the conclusions about the deviations of the dynamics
from the averaged system (Subsection
4.10
).

Remark 4.1
.

Throughout this section, as well as in the appendices we refer to for the
proofs (actually from Appendix
B
on), we assume
\rho
to be such
that Hypotheses
1
to
3
hold
(see Remark
2.13
) and
we call
C
any constant independent of
\rho
whose numerical value is not relevant.

4.1
The extended map

Even though the set
\Omega
is positively invariant for the map
{\mathscr{S}}
in (
2.16
),
it is useful to extend the map
{\mathscr{S}}|_{\Omega}
outside
\Omega
.
To do this, we proceed as follows:

•

let
\chi_{\rm ext}\!:\mathds{R}\to\mathds{R}
be a
C^{\infty}
function such that

\chi_{\rm ext}(x)=1
for
x\leq 0
and
\chi_{\rm ext}(x)=0
for
x\geq 1
while

\partial_{x}\chi_{\rm ext}(x)\leq 0
for every
x\in\mathds{R}
;

•

set
s_{M}:=\min\{1,\nu_{M}/2\}
,
where
\nu_{M}:=\inf_{\psi\in\mathds{T}^{2}}|f(\varphi_{M},\psi)|
;

•

define
f_{\rm ext}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{\alpha_{0},6}(\mathds{R}\times\mathds{T}^{2},\mathds{R})
,
by setting
f_{\rm ext}(\varphi,\psi)=f(\varphi,\psi)
for
\varphi_{m}\leq\varphi\leq\varphi_{M}

while

f_{\rm ext}(\varphi,\psi)=\biggl(\sum_{i=0}^{6}\partial_{\varphi}^{i}f(\varphi_{M},\psi)(\varphi-\varphi_{M})^{i}\biggr)\chi_{\rm ext}\left(\frac{\varphi-\varphi_{M}}{s_{M}}\right)-\frac{\nu_{M}}{2}\left(1-\chi_{\rm ext}\left(\frac{\varphi-\varphi_{M}}{s_{M}}\right)\right),\vskip-8.5359pt

for
\varphi>\varphi_{M}
, and an analogous expression for
\varphi<\varphi_{m}
.

One has
|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|f_{\rm ext}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{\alpha_{0},6}\leq s_{M}^{-6}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|\chi_{\rm ext}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,6}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|F|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{\alpha_{0},6}

and
\inf_{(\varphi,\psi)\in\mathds{R}\times\mathds{T}^{2}\backslash\Lambda_{r}}|f_{\rm ext}(\varphi,\psi)|\geq\frac{1}{2}\inf_{(\varphi,\psi)\in\Omega\backslash\Lambda_{r}}|f(\varphi,\psi)|

for any
r>0
.
Moreover the map

{\mathscr{S}}_{\rm ext}(\varphi,\psi):=(\varphi+\rho f_{\rm ext}(\varphi,\psi),A_{0}\psi)

is defined on
\mathds{R}\times\mathds{T}^{2}
, coincides with
{\mathscr{S}}(\varphi,\psi)
for

(\varphi,\psi)\in\Omega
and, restricted to any
\Omega_{\rm ext}={\mathcal{U}}_{\rm ext}\times\mathds{T}^{2}
, with
{\mathcal{U}}_{\rm ext}\supseteq{\mathcal{U}}
a closed interval,
satisfies Hypotheses
1
–
3
with
\Omega_{\rm ext}
instead of
\Omega
.
Extending
{\mathscr{S}}(\varphi,\psi)
on
\Omega_{\rm ext}
naturally defines a map
{\mathscr{S}}_{\rm 1,ext}(\theta,\psi):={\mathscr{S}}_{\rm ext}(\theta+W(\psi),\psi)-W(A_{0}\psi)
on

\Omega_{1,{\rm ext}}:=\{(\theta,\psi)\in\mathds{R}\times\mathds{T}^{2}:(\theta+W(\psi),\psi)\in\Omega_{\rm ext}\}
.
In the following discussion we need to choose
\Omega_{\rm ext}
in such a way
that
\Omega\subset\Omega_{1,{\rm ext}}
(see Subsection
4.8
).
The conjugation
{\mathcal{H}}
as well can be extended to a function
{\mathcal{H}}_{\rm ext}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{\alpha_{0},1}(\Omega_{\rm ext},\mathds{R})
,
by reasoning as in Subsection
3.2
with

{\mathscr{S}}_{\rm ext}
instead of
{\mathscr{S}}
everywhere.

Remark 4.2
.

The reason why we need to extend
{\mathscr{S}}|_{\Omega}
outside
\Omega
to a map

{\mathscr{S}}_{\rm ext}
, potentially different from the original
{\mathscr{S}}
on

(\mathds{T}\times\mathds{T}^{2})\backslash\Omega
, is that we aim to compare
{\mathscr{S}}
with other maps
which, albeit being constructed starting from
{\mathscr{S}}
,
not only may fail to admit
\Omega
as an invariant set (see
Remark
4.6
), but also are not necessarily defined in the whole
\Omega
.
In order not to discuss separately the dynamics near the boundary of
\Omega
, it turns out to be easier to extend the maps to a larger domain

\Omega_{\rm ext}
in such a way that they satisfy automatically the same properties as
{\mathscr{S}}
.
We stress here that all the functions appearing in Theorems
4
to
8
depend
only on
{\mathscr{S}}|_{\Omega}
and, when they are estimated, the errors introduced as an effect of
the arbitrariness of the extensions are under control (see Remark
4.14
).

4.2
A correlation inequality

This subsection is dedicated to a generalization of Proposition
2.2
that
plays a central role in the proof of Theorem
4
and Lemma
2.25
.

For any two given sets of functions
{\mathfrak{v}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathfrak{v}}_{n-1}

and

{\mathfrak{w}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathfrak{w}}_{n-1}
on
\mathds{T}^{2}
,
set
{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}={\mathfrak{v}}_{i}+\rho\,{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}
for
i=0,\ldots,n-1
. Define, for
k=0,\ldots,n

and
i=0,\ldots,n-k
,

{\mathfrak{u}}^{(k)}_{i}(\psi):=\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}{\mathfrak{u}}_{i+j}(A_{0}^{j}\psi),\qquad{\mathfrak{u}}^{(k)}(\psi):={\mathfrak{u}}^{(k)}_{0}(\psi),\qquad{\mathfrak{u}}^{(-k)}(\psi):=\prod_{j=1}^{k}{\mathfrak{u}}_{k-j}(A_{0}^{-j}\psi)={\mathfrak{u}}^{(k)}{\circ}A_{0}^{-k},\vskip-5.69046pt

(4.1)

with
{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}{\vphantom{-}}^{(0)}(\psi)=1
according to Remark
2.24
,
and define similarly
{\mathfrak{v}}^{(k)}_{i}(\psi)
,
{\mathfrak{v}}^{(k)}(\psi)
and
{\mathfrak{v}}^{(-k)}(\psi)
.

We write

{\mathfrak{u}}^{(k)}_{i}\!\!-\!{\mathfrak{v}}^{(k)}_{i}=\rho\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(k-1)}_{i}\,{\mathfrak{w}}_{i+k-1}{\circ}A_{0}^{k-1}+({\mathfrak{u}}^{(k-1)}_{i}\!\!-\!{\mathfrak{v}}^{(k-1)}_{i}){\mathfrak{v}}_{i+k-1}{\circ}A_{0}^{k-1}
,
so that, iterating, we get

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{u}}^{(k)}_{i}={\mathfrak{v}}^{(k)}_{i}

\displaystyle+\rho\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}{\mathfrak{u}}^{(j)}_{i}\,{\mathfrak{w}}_{i+j}{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,{\mathfrak{v}}^{(k-j-1)}_{i+j+1}{\circ}A_{0}^{j+1}={\mathfrak{v}}^{(k)}_{i}+\rho\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}{\mathfrak{v}}^{(j)}_{i}\,{\mathfrak{w}}_{i+j}{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,{\mathfrak{v}}^{(k-j-1)}_{i+j+1}{\circ}A_{0}^{j+1}

(4.2)

\displaystyle+\rho^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{j-1}{\mathfrak{u}}^{(j^{\prime})}_{i}\,{\mathfrak{w}}_{i+j^{\prime}}{\circ}A_{0}^{j^{\prime}}\,{\mathfrak{v}}^{(j-j^{\prime}-1)}_{i+j^{\prime}+1}{\circ}A_{0}^{j^{\prime}+1}\,{\mathfrak{w}}_{i+j}{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,{\mathfrak{v}}^{(k-j-1)}_{i+j+1}{\circ}A_{0}^{j+1}.

where the second equality follows applying the first equality to the
factor
{\mathfrak{u}}^{(j)}_{i}
in the first line;
according to the convention in Remark
2.24
,
the sum in the second line of (
4.2
) vanishes for
k=0
, while the sum in the last line vanishes for
k=0,1
.
We can also proceed “in the opposite direction” to get

{\mathfrak{u}}^{(k)}_{i}={\mathfrak{v}}^{(k)}_{i}+\rho\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}{\mathfrak{v}}^{(j)}_{i}\,{\mathfrak{w}}_{i+j}{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(k-j-1)}_{i+j+1}{\circ}A_{0}^{j+1},\vskip-2.84544pt

(4.3)

where we avoid writing the equivalent of the second expansion in (
4.2
) since we will not need it.

The following result plays a key role in the forthcoming
analysis. The proof is based on (
4.2
).

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
{\mathfrak{u}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathfrak{u}}_{n-1}
be any functions in

{\mathfrak{B}}_{\alpha}(\mathds{T}^{2},\mathds{R})
, with
\alpha\in(0,1]
, such that

1.

0<\langle{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}\rangle\leq\|{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1-\rho\,\gamma
for all
i=0,\ldots,n-1
,

2.

\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}=O(\rho)
for all
i=0,\ldots,n-1
,

and set
\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{(n)}:=\langle{\mathfrak{u}}_{0}\rangle\langle{\mathfrak{u}}_{1}\rangle\ldots\langle{\mathfrak{u}}_{n-1}\rangle
.
Given
g_{+}\in{\mathfrak{B}}^{+}_{\alpha}(\mathds{T}^{2},\mathds{R})
and
g_{-}\in{\mathfrak{B}}^{-}_{\alpha}(\mathds{T}^{2},\mathds{R})
, one has

\displaystyle\Bigl|\langle g_{+}\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}{\circ}A_{0}\,g_{-}{\circ}A_{0}^{n+1}\rangle-\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{(n)}\langle g_{+}\rangle\langle g_{-}\rangle\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C(1\!-\!\rho\,\gamma)^{n}\Bigl((1\!+\!\alpha n)\lambda^{-\alpha n}\|\tilde{g}_{+}\|_{\alpha}^{+}\|\tilde{g}_{-}\|_{\alpha}^{-}+\rho\bigl(\|\tilde{g}_{+}\|_{\alpha}^{+}\|g_{-}\|_{\alpha}^{-}+\|g_{+}\|_{\alpha}^{+}\|\tilde{g}_{-}\|_{\alpha}^{-}\bigr)+\rho^{2}n|\langle g_{+}\rangle||\langle g_{-}\rangle|\Bigr).

Proof.

For any function
{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}
, with
i=0,\ldots,n-1
,
let
{\mathfrak{v}}_{i}:=\langle{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}\rangle
and
\rho\,{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}:=\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}

and introduce the notation
\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{(k)}_{i}:=\langle{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}\rangle\ldots\langle{\mathfrak{u}}_{k-1}\rangle
,
so that
\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{(k)}=\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{(k)}_{0}
.
Then we write

\langle g_{+}\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}{\circ}A_{0}\,g_{-}{\circ}A_{0}^{n+1}\rangle=\langle g_{+}\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}{\circ}A_{0}\,\tilde{g}_{-}{\circ}A_{0}^{n+1}\rangle+\langle\tilde{g}_{+}\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}{\circ}A_{0}\rangle\langle g_{-}\rangle+\langle g_{+}\rangle\langle{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}\rangle\langle g_{-}\rangle,

(4.4)

so that, using the first line of (
4.2
), with
{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}{\circ}A_{0}
instead of
{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}
,
we rewrite the first term in (
4.4
) as

\langle g_{+}\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}{\circ}A_{0}\,\tilde{g}_{-}{\circ}A_{0}^{n+1}\rangle=\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{n}\langle g_{+}\,\tilde{g}_{-}{\circ}A_{0}^{n+1}\rangle+\rho\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{n-k-1}_{k+1}\langle g_{+}\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(k)}{\circ}A_{0}\,\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}{\circ}A_{0}^{k+1}\,\tilde{g}_{-}{\circ}A_{0}^{n+1}\rangle.\vskip-2.84544pt

Using (
2.4a
) and (
2.21
), we get

\displaystyle\bigl|g_{+}{\circ}A_{0}^{-(k+1)}{\mathfrak{u}}^{(k)}\!{\circ}\!A_{0}^{-k}\,\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}\bigr|_{\alpha}^{+}

\displaystyle\leq\|g_{+}{\circ}A_{0}^{-(k+1)}\,\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}\bigr\|_{\infty}\bigl|{\mathfrak{u}}^{(k)}{\circ}A_{0}^{-k}\bigr|_{\alpha}^{+}+\bigl|g_{+}{\circ}A_{0}^{-(k+1)}\,\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}\bigr|_{\alpha}^{+}\|{\mathfrak{u}}^{(k)}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{k}\|g_{+}{\circ}A_{0}^{-(k+1)}\,\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}\|_{\alpha}^{+},

so that, from (
2.7
) and Proposition
2.2
, we obtain in
(
4.4
)

\bigl|\langle g_{+}\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}{\circ}A_{0}\,\tilde{g}_{-}{\circ}A_{0}^{n+1}\rangle\bigr|\leq C(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n}\lambda^{-\alpha n}(1+\alpha n)\|\tilde{g}_{+}\|_{\alpha}^{+}\|\tilde{g}_{-}\|_{\alpha}^{-}+C\rho(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n}\|g_{+}\|_{\alpha}^{+}\|\tilde{g}_{-}\|_{\alpha}^{-}\,.

(4.5)

Analogously to (
4.5
), using (
4.3
), with
{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}{\circ}A_{0}

instead of
{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}
, and Proposition
2.2
, we get

\displaystyle\bigl|\langle\tilde{g}_{+}\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}{\circ}A_{0}\rangle\bigr|\leq\rho\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{k}_{0}|\langle\tilde{g}_{+}\,\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}{\circ}A_{0}^{k}\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n-k-1)}{\circ}A_{0}^{k+1}\rangle\,|\leq C\rho(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n}\|\tilde{g}_{+}\|_{\alpha}^{+}\,.

(4.6)

Finally, for
n\geq 2
, since
\langle\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}\rangle=0
for all
k=0,\ldots,n-1
,
using the third line of (
4.2
),
once more with
{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}{\circ}A_{0}
instead of
{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl|\langle\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}\rangle-\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{n}\bigr|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\rho^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{k-j-1}_{j+1}\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{n-k-1}_{k+1}\langle{\mathfrak{u}}^{(j)}{\circ}A_{0}^{-j}\,\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{j}\,\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}{\circ}A_{0}^{k-j}\rangle\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}j^{2}\lambda^{-\alpha j}(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n}\|\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{j}\|_{\alpha}^{+}\|\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}\|_{\alpha}^{-}\leq C\rho^{2}n(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n-2}\|\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{j}\|_{\alpha}^{+}\|\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}\|_{\alpha}^{-},

where we have used Proposition
2.2
and (
4.1
), so that we obtain

\bigl|\langle\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}\rangle-\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{n}_{0}\bigr|\leq C\rho^{2}n(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n}\,.

(4.7)

Collecting all contributions (
4.5
)–(
4.7
) gives the thesis. ∎

Remark 4.4
.

The factor
\rho\bigl(\|\tilde{g}_{+}\|_{\alpha}^{+}\|g_{-}\|_{\alpha}^{-}+\|g_{+}\|_{\alpha}^{+}\|\tilde{g}_{-}\|_{\alpha}^{-}\bigr)

appearing in the estimate of Proposition
4.3
, in principle, might be replaced more pragmatically with

2\rho\,\|g_{+}\|_{\alpha}^{+}\|g_{-}\|_{\alpha}^{-}
. However, it may be useful in some cases. In particular, for
g_{+}=g_{-}=1
, we obtain

|\langle{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}\rangle-\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{(n)}|\leq C\rho^{2}n(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n}
and hence

|\langle{\mathfrak{u}}^{(n)}\rangle-\langle{\mathfrak{u}}\rangle^{(n)}|\leq C\rho(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})^{n}
for any
\gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\gamma)
,
with the constant
C
depending on
\gamma^{\prime}
.

4.3
Oscillations of the invariant manifold: proof of Theorem
4

Following Remark
2.7
, we set
b(\psi):=f(0,\psi)
and
v(\psi):=\partial_{\varphi}f(0,\psi)
, with
\|v\|_{\infty}\leq C
by Remark
2.13
,
and write

f(\varphi,\psi)=b(\psi)+v(\psi)\,\varphi+d(\varphi,\psi)\varphi^{2},

(4.8)

which implicitly defines the function
d(\varphi,\psi)
. We also set

\mu(\psi):=1+\rho v(\psi)=1+\rho\partial_{\varphi}f(0,\psi)

and, for
i\geq 0
, according to (
4.1
),

\mu^{(i)}(\psi)=\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\mu(A_{0}^{j}\psi),\qquad\qquad\mu^{(-i)}(\psi)=\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mu(A_{0}^{-j}\psi)=\mu^{(i)}(A^{-i}\psi).\vskip-2.84544pt

(4.9)

Decomposing
\mu(\psi)=\langle\mu\rangle+\tilde{\mu}(\psi)
according to (
2.1
), we have

\langle\mu\rangle=\overline{\mu}
, with
\overline{\mu}
as in (
2.19
), and

\tilde{\mu}(\psi)=\rho\tilde{v}(\psi)
. We get
\langle\mu\rangle\leq\|\mu\|_{\infty}\leq 1-\rho\,\gamma
, since

\partial_{\varphi}f(\varphi,\psi)\leq-\gamma
for all
\varphi\in[S_{m},S_{M}]
and
\bar{\varphi}=0\in[S_{m},S_{M}]

(see the definition of
\gamma
after (
2.16
) and Remark
2.6
) and
|\mu|_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}=O(\rho)
.

In Section
3.1
we proved that
W=\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathscr{E}}^{n}[0]
uniformly on
\mathds{T}^{2}
,
with
{\mathscr{E}}
defined in (
3.2
). Thus, to prove (
2.24
), we show that, for all
n\in\mathds{N}
,
one has

\big|\langle{\mathscr{E}}^{n}[0]\rangle\bigr|\leq C\rho

and

\langle({\mathscr{E}}^{n}[0])^{2}\rangle\leq C\rho
,
for some constant
C
independent of
n
.
To this end, analogously to (
3.2
), we set

{\mathscr{E}}_{0}[W](\psi):=G_{0}(W(A_{0}^{-1}\psi),A_{0}^{-1}\psi),\qquad G_{0}(\varphi,\psi):=\varphi+\rho f_{0}(\varphi,\psi),\qquad f_{0}(\varphi,\psi):=b(\psi)+v(\psi)\varphi\vskip-2.84544pt

and observe that, for any
h:\mathds{T}^{2}\to\mathds{R}
,

{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[h]=\rho\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mu^{(-i+1)}b{\circ}A_{0}^{-i}+\mu^{(-n)}h{\circ}A_{0}^{-n}.\vskip-2.84544pt

(4.10)

Remark 4.5
.

Since
{\mathscr{E}}_{0}
is a contraction on the space of bounded continuous
functions from
\mathds{T}^{2}
to
\mathds{R}
, the fixed point equation

{\mathscr{E}}_{0}[W]=W
admits a unique solution
W_{0}
and
{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[0]

converges uniformly to
W_{0}
. We can write

W_{0}=\rho\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mu^{(-i+1)}b{\circ}A_{0}^{-i}+\mu^{(-n)}W_{0}{\circ}A_{0}^{-n}=\rho\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\mu^{(-i+1)}b{\circ}A_{0}^{-i}\,,\vskip-2.84544pt

(4.11)

from which we get
\|W_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma^{-1}\|b\|_{\infty}
,
while
|W_{0}|_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}=O(\rho)
by (
2.8
).

To prove Theorem
4
we show, first, by explicit computation,
that
|\langle W_{0}\rangle|=O(\rho)
and
\langle W_{0}^{2}\rangle=O(\rho)
, then that

\langle|W-W_{0}|\rangle=O(\rho)
, by comparing the iterates of
{\mathscr{E}}_{0}
and of
{\mathscr{E}}
and using their contractivity.

From the last definition in (
4.1
) we get

\langle\mu^{(-i+1)}b{\circ}A_{0}^{-i}\rangle=\langle b\,\mu^{(i-1)}{\circ}A_{0}\rangle
. Since, by definition,
\langle b\rangle=0
, using Proposition
4.3
,
with
n=i-1
,
{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}=\mu
for
k=0,\ldots,n-1
,
g_{+}=b
and
g_{-}=1
, we find

\bigl|\langle{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[0]\rangle\bigr|\leq C\rho^{2}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{i}\|b\|_{\alpha_{0}}\leq C\rho\,.\vskip-8.5359pt

Moreover we have

\vskip-5.69046pt({\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[0])^{2}=\rho^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mu^{(-i+1)})^{2}(b{\circ}A_{0}^{-i})^{2}\\
+2\rho^{2}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}(\mu^{(-i+1)})^{2}\,b{\circ}A_{0}^{-i}\,\mu{\circ}A_{0}^{-i}\,\mu^{-(j-i-1)}{\circ}A_{0}^{-i}\,b{\circ}A_{0}^{-j},\vskip-2.84544pt

where

\bigl\langle(\mu^{(-i+1)})^{2}\,b{\circ}A_{0}^{-i}\,\mu{\circ}A_{0}^{-i}\,\mu^{(-j+i+1)}{\circ}A_{0}^{-i}\,b{\circ}A_{0}^{-j}\big\rangle=\bigl\langle b\,\mu^{(j-i-1)}{\circ}A_{0}(\mu^{(i-1)})^{2}{\circ}A_{0}^{j-i+1}\,b{\circ}A_{0}^{j-i}\,\mu{\circ}A_{0}^{j-i}\bigr\rangle,\vskip-2.84544pt

so that, first using (
2.7
) and (
2.8
) to estimate

\bigl|(\mu^{(i-1)})^{2}{\circ}A_{0}\,b\,\mu\bigr|_{\alpha_{0}}^{-}\leq\bigl|(\mu^{(i-1)})^{2}{\circ}A_{0}\bigr|_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}\bigl\|b\,\mu\bigr\|_{\infty}+\bigl\|(\mu^{(i-1)})^{2}\bigr\|_{\infty}\bigl|b\,\mu\bigr|_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}\leq C(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{2(i-1)},\vskip-2.84544pt

then applying once more Proposition
4.3
,
with
n=j-i-1
,
{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}=\mu
for
k=0,\ldots,n-1
,

g_{+}=b
and
g_{-}=(\mu^{(i-1)})^{2}{\circ}A_{0}\,b\,\mu
,
we get

\displaystyle\langle{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[0]^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C\rho\|b\|_{\infty}^{2}+C\rho^{3}\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\!\!\!\!(1\!-\!\rho\gamma)^{j-i}\|b\|_{\alpha_{0}}\|(\mu^{(i-1)})^{2}{\circ}A_{0}\,b\,\mu\|_{\alpha_{0}}^{-}\leq C\rho+C\rho^{3}\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}^{n}\!\!\!\!\!(1\!-\!\rho\gamma)^{j+i}\leq C\rho.

Thus, if
W_{0}
is defined as in Remark
4.5
, we obtain

\langle W_{0}\rangle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[0]\rangle=O(\rho),\qquad\langle W_{0}^{2}\rangle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[0]^{2}\rangle=O(\rho).\vskip-2.84544pt

(4.12)

For any
n\geq 0
, we can write

{\mathscr{E}}^{n}[0]\!-\!{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[0]=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}{\mathscr{E}}^{n-i}[{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{i}[0]]\!-\!{\mathscr{E}}^{n-i-1}[{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{i+1}[0]]=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}{\mathscr{E}}^{n-i-1}[{\mathscr{E}}[{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{i}[0]]]\!-\!{\mathscr{E}}^{n-i-1}[{\mathscr{E}}_{0}[{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{i}[0]]]\,.\vskip-2.84544pt

(4.13)

Remark 4.6
.

It may happen that
{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{i}[0]\not\subset\Omega
for some
i
.
In such a case, we observe that there exists an interval
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}:=[\phi_{m}^{\prime},\phi_{M}^{\prime}]\supset{\mathcal{U}}
such that
\Omega^{\prime}:={\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\times\mathds{T}^{2}

and
{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[0](\psi)\in\Omega^{\prime}
for all
n\geq 0
and all
\psi\in\mathds{T}^{2}
.
Thus for the r.h.s. of (
4.13
) to be well defined, we
compute
{\mathscr{E}}
by replacing the map
{\mathscr{S}}
with the extension
{\mathscr{S}}_{\rm ext}

introduced in Subsection
4.1
such that
\Omega_{\rm ext}\supset\Omega^{\prime}

(see also Remark
4.2
).

By taking
{\mathscr{S}}_{\rm ext}
according to Remark
4.6
and reasoning as in the proof of Lemma

2.5
, we find that for any
r>0
there exists
N^{\prime}_{r}\leq\max\{\phi_{M}^{\prime}-S_{M},S_{m}-\phi_{m}^{\prime}\}/(\rho\inf_{\Omega^{\prime}\backslash\Lambda_{r}}|f_{\rm ext}(\varphi,\psi)|)

such that
{\mathscr{S}}_{\rm ext}^{N_{r}^{\prime}}(\Omega^{\prime})\subset\Lambda_{r}
,
and, given
W_{1},W_{2}:\!\mathds{T}^{2}\to[\phi^{\prime}_{m},\phi^{\prime}_{M}]
, we have
{\mathscr{E}}^{N^{\prime}_{r}}[W_{i}]\in[S_{m}-r,S_{M}+r]
for
i=1,2
, while

|{\mathscr{E}}^{N^{\prime}_{r}}[W_{1}]-{\mathscr{E}}^{N^{\prime}_{r}}[W_{2}]|\leq(1+\rho\|\partial_{\varphi}f\|_{\infty})^{N^{\prime}_{r}}|W_{1}-W_{2}|
.
Thus, for any
\gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\gamma)
, with the notation in property
4
of Lemma
2.5
,
we can choose
r=r(\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})
and obtain
N_{r}^{\prime}=O(1/\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})
and,
for any
W_{1},W_{2}\in[S_{m}-r,S_{M}+r]
and any
k\geq 0
, we may bound

|{\mathscr{E}}^{k}[W_{1}]-{\mathscr{E}}^{k}[W_{2}]|\leq(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})^{k}|W_{1}-W_{2}|
.
Summing up, (
4.13
) gives

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{E}}^{n}[0](\psi)\!-\!{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[0](\psi)|\!\leq\!\left(\frac{1+\rho\|\partial_{\varphi}f\|_{\infty}}{1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime}}\right)^{\!N_{r}^{\prime}}\!\!\rho\!\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left(1\!-\!\rho\,\gamma^{\prime}\right)^{n-i-1}\!\left|d({\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{i}[0](A_{0}^{-1}\psi),A_{0}^{-1}\psi)\right|({\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{i}[0](A_{0}^{-1}\psi))^{2},

with
d(\varphi,\psi)
implicitly defined in (
4.8
).
Integrating over
\psi
and using the bound on
\langle{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[0]^{2}\rangle
,
we obtain

\langle|{\mathscr{E}}^{n}[0]-{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{n}[0]|\rangle\leq C\rho\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime}\right)^{i}\|d\|_{\infty}\langle{\mathscr{E}}_{0}^{i}[0]^{2}\rangle\leq C\rho,

which yields

\left\langle|W-W_{0}|\right\rangle\leq C\rho,\vskip 2.84544pt

(4.14)

which, together with (
4.12
), gives

|\langle W\rangle|\leq|\langle W-W_{0}\rangle|+|\langle W_{0}\rangle|\leq\langle|W-W_{0}|\rangle+|\langle W_{0}\rangle|\leq C\rho
,
that is the first bound in (
2.24
).
Finally, we have
|\langle W^{2}-W_{0}^{2}\rangle|\leq\left\langle|W-W_{0}|\;|W+W_{0}|\right\rangle\leq C\rho
,
because of (
4.14
),
and hence, thanks to (
4.12
),

\langle W^{2}\rangle\leq|\langle W^{2}-W_{0}^{2}\rangle|+\langle W_{0}^{2}\rangle\leq C\rho
,
that is the second bound in (
2.24
).

4.4
Fluctuations of the linearized dynamics: proof of Lemma
2.25

Having studied the oscillations of the invariant manifold we now focus our
attention on the linearized dynamics.
Using that
\kappa(\psi)=1+\rho\,\partial_{\varphi}f(W(\psi),\psi)
and
\mu(\psi)=1+\rho\,\partial_{\varphi}f(0,\psi)=1-\rho\,v(\psi)
, we write

\kappa(\psi)=\mu(\psi)+\rho\,\xi(\psi),\qquad\xi(\psi):=d_{1}(\psi)\,W(\psi)+d_{2}(\psi)\,W^{2}(\psi),\qquad d_{1}(\psi):=\partial_{\varphi}^{2}f(0,\psi),

(4.15)

which implicitly defines the function
d_{2}\in{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}_{\alpha_{-},\alpha_{+}}\!(\mathds{T}^{2},\mathds{R})
.

From Proposition
4.3
, with
g_{+}=g_{-}=1
and either
{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}=\mu
or
{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}=\mu^{2}
for all
i
,
and Remark
4.4
, it follows that

\bigl|\langle\mu^{(n)}-\overline{\mu}^{n}\rangle\bigr|\leq C\rho^{2}n(1-\rho\gamma)^{n}
and
\big\langle{(\mu^{(n)}-\overline{\mu}^{n})^{2}}\big\rangle\leq C\rho^{2}n(1-\rho\gamma)^{2n}
,
and hence, in order to prove (
2.27
), it is
enough to show that

\bigl|\langle\kappa^{(n)}-\mu^{(n)}\rangle\bigr|\leq C\rho(1-\rho\gamma)^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{2}\rho^{k}n^{k},\qquad\big\langle{(\kappa^{(n)}-\mu^{(n)})^{2}}\big\rangle\leq C\rho(1-\rho\gamma)^{2n}\sum_{k=0}^{2}\rho^{k}n^{k}.

(4.16)

Remark 4.7
.

The proof of (
4.16
) represents a first instance of the strategy
outlined in Section
2.6
. In order to bypass the low
regularity of
\kappa
due to its dependence on the invariant
manifold, we expand it up to the second order in
W
so as to use Theorem

4
to estimate the average of the quadratic contributions, the
bound (
4.14
) to reduce the analysis of the average of the linear
contributions to the more manageable function
W_{0}
and the
explicit expression for
W_{0}
in (
4.11
) to obtain the desired
estimates.

Thus, we proceed with the proof of (
4.16
). By Remark (
4.2
) we can write

\displaystyle\kappa^{(n)}-\mu^{(n)}=\rho\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\kappa^{(j)}\,\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,\mu^{(n-j-1)}{\circ}A^{j+1}

(4.17)

\displaystyle=\rho\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\mu^{(j)}\,\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,\mu^{(n-j-1)}{\circ}A^{j+1}+\rho^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}\kappa^{(k)}\,\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{k}\,\mu^{(j-k-1)}{\circ}A_{0}^{k+1}\,\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,\mu^{(n-j-1)}{\circ}A_{0}^{j+1}.

Since

|\langle\kappa^{(k)}\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{k}\mu^{(j-k-1)}{\circ}A_{0}^{k+1}\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\mu^{(n-j-1)}{\circ}A_{0}^{j+1}\rangle|\leq(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n-2}\langle|\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{k}\,\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{j}|\rangle\leq(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n-2}\langle\xi^{2}\rangle

and
\langle\xi^{2}\rangle\leq C\rho
, by (
4.15
) and (
2.24
), eventually we get, for
n\geq 2
,

\biggl|\sum_{j=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}\left\langle\kappa^{(k)}\,\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{k}\,\mu^{(j-k-1)}{\circ}A_{0}^{k+1}\,\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,\mu^{(n-j-1)}A_{0}^{j+1}\right\rangle\biggr|\leq C\rho\,n^{2}(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n}.\vskip-2.84544pt

On the other hand, since we have, again by (
2.24
),

\bigl|\bigl\langle\mu^{(j)}\,(d_{2}W^{2}){\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,\mu^{(n-j-1)}{\circ}A^{j+1}\bigr\rangle\bigl|\leq(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n-1}\|d_{2}\|_{\infty}\langle W^{2}\rangle\leq C\rho(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n}.

we need to estimate

\langle\mu^{(j)}\,(d_{1}W){\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,\mu^{(n-j-1)}{\circ}A_{0}^{j+1}\rangle\!=\!\langle\mu^{(j)}{\circ}A_{0}^{-j}\,d_{1}W\,\mu^{(n-j-1)}{\circ}A_{0}\rangle.

By Proposition
4.3
,
with
\alpha\!=\!\alpha_{+}
,
n\!-\!j\!-\!1
instead of
n
,
{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}\!=\!\mu
for
i\!=\!0,\ldots,n\!-\!j\!-\!2
,
g_{+}=\mu^{(j)}{\circ}A_{0}^{-j}\,d_{1}W
and
g_{-}\!=\!1
, we find

\bigl|\bigl\langle\mu^{(j)}\,(d_{1}W){\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,\mu^{(n-j-1)}{\circ}A_{0}^{j+1}\bigr\rangle\bigr|\leq C(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n-j}\bigl|\bigl\langle\mu^{(j)\bigl\rangle{\circ}A_{0}^{-j}\,d_{1}W}\bigr|+C\rho\,(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{n}\,(1+\rho\,(n-j)),

where we have used also that
\|W\|_{\alpha_{+}}^{+}\leq C
and
\|\mu^{(j)}{\circ}A_{0}^{-j}\|_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}\leq C
,
because of Theorem
2
and (
2.8
), respectively.
Then, by (
4.14
) and Remark
4.5
, we find

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mu^{(j)}{\circ}A_{0}^{-j}d_{1}W\right\rangle\right|=\left|\left\langle\mu^{(j)}\,(d_{1}W){\circ}A_{0}^{j}\right\rangle\right|\leq\left|\left\langle\mu^{(j)}\,(d_{1}W_{0}){\circ}A_{0}^{j}\right\rangle\right|+C\rho(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{j}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left\langle W_{0}\,(\mu^{(j)})^{2}\,d_{1}{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\right\rangle\right|+\rho\!\sum_{k=1}^{j}\left|\left\langle\mu^{(j-k)}b{\circ}A_{0}^{j-k}(\mu^{(k-1)})^{2}{\circ}A_{0}^{j-k}\mu{\circ}A_{0}^{j-1}d_{1}{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\right\rangle\right|+C\rho(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{j},

where, since
\langle W_{0}\rangle=O(\rho)
, we have

|\langle W_{0}\,(\mu^{(j)})^{2}\,d_{1}{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\rangle|\leq C\,(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{2j}\,(1+\alpha_{0}j)\lambda^{-\alpha_{0}j}+\rho+\rho^{3}j)
,
by Proposition
4.3
, with
n=j
,
{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}=\mu^{2}
for
i=0,\ldots,j-1
,
g_{+}=W_{0}{\circ}A_{0}^{-1}
and
g_{-}=d_{1}
,
while

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mu^{(j-k)}b{\circ}A_{0}^{j-k}(\mu^{(k-1)})^{2}{\circ}A_{0}^{j-k}\mu{\circ}A_{0}^{j-1}\,d_{1}{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{j-k}\left|\left\langle b(\mu^{(k-1)})^{2}\,\mu{\circ}A_{0}^{k-1}\,d_{1}{\circ}A_{0}^{k}\right\rangle\right|+C\rho(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{j+k}\,(1+\rho\,(j-k))

\displaystyle\leq C(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{j-k}\bigl((1+\alpha_{0}k)\lambda^{-\alpha_{0}k}+\rho\bigr)\|b\|_{\alpha_{0}}\|d_{1}\|_{\alpha_{0}}+C\rho(1-\rho\,\gamma)^{j+k},

where we have used twice Proposition
4.3
, first
with
n=j-k
,
{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}=\mu
for
i=0,\ldots,j-k-1
,
g_{+}=1
and
g_{-}=b\,(\mu^{(k-1)})^{2}\mu{\circ}A_{0}^{k-1}d_{1}{\circ}A_{0}^{k}
,
then with
n=k
,
{\mathfrak{u}}_{i}=\mu^{2}
for
i=0,\ldots,k-1
and
{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}=\mu
,
g_{+}=b
and
g_{-}=d_{1}
.
Inserting all the bounds into (
4.17
), we obtain the first of (
4.16
).

For the second of (
2.27
), we use the first line of (
4.17
) to write

\displaystyle\left\langle\!\bigl(\kappa^{(n)}\!-\!\mu^{(n)}\bigr)^{\!2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\!\rho^{2}\!\!\sum_{i,j=1}^{n-1}\!\left|\left\langle\kappa^{(i)}\,\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{i}\,\mu^{(n\!-\!i\!-\!1)}{\circ}A^{i+1}\kappa^{(j)}\,\xi{\circ}A_{0}^{j}\,\mu^{(n\!-\!j\!-\!1)}{\circ}A^{j+1}\right\rangle\right|\leq C\,n^{2}\rho^{2}\left(1\!-\!\rho\,\gamma\right)^{2(n-1)}\!\left\langle\xi^{2}\right\rangle.

Thus, the second bound in (
4.16
) follows using again the second bound in (
2.24
).

4.5
Iterated products

We introduce here some notation that will be used widely throughout the rest of the paper.
Let
{\mathscr{S}}
be any map on
{\mathcal{U}}\times\mathds{T}^{2}
of the form

{\mathscr{S}}(\varphi,\psi)=({\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi}(\varphi,\psi),{\mathscr{S}}_{\psi}(\varphi,\psi)):=(G(\varphi,\psi),A_{0}\psi)\,,\vskip-2.84544pt

(4.18)

and let
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{n-1}
be functions defined in

{\mathcal{U}}\times\mathds{T}^{2}
.
Define, for
k=0,\ldots,n
and
i=0,\ldots,n-k
,

{\mathfrak{p}}^{(k)}_{[i]}({\mathscr{S}};\varphi,\psi):=\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i+j}({\mathscr{S}}^{j}(\varphi,\psi)),\qquad\qquad{\mathfrak{p}}^{(k)}({\mathscr{S}};\varphi,\psi):={\mathfrak{p}}^{(k)}_{[0]}({\mathscr{S}};\varphi,\psi),\vskip-5.69046pt

(4.19)

and set
{\mathfrak{p}}^{(-1)}_{[i]}({\mathscr{S}};\varphi,\psi)={\mathfrak{p}}^{(0)}_{[i]}({\mathscr{S}};\varphi,\psi)=1
according to the convention established in Remark
2.24
.

Remark 4.8
.

The map
{\mathscr{S}}
in (
4.18
) is not necessarily the map (
2.9
) which defines our model. In particular, in what follows,
we shall use the notation (
4.19
) for several maps, including the translated map
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
and the auxiliary map

{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
which will be introduced in Subsection
4.8.1
.

If the function
{\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi}(\varphi,\psi)
does not depend on
\psi
and the functions

{\mathfrak{p}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{n-1}
are independent of
\psi
as well, instead of
(
4.19
) we may consider

{\mathfrak{p}}^{(k)}_{[i]}({\mathscr{S}};\varphi):=\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i+j}(G^{j}(\varphi)),\qquad\qquad{\mathfrak{p}}^{(k)}({\mathscr{S}};\varphi):={\mathfrak{p}}^{(k)}_{[0]}({\mathscr{S}};\varphi),\vskip-5.69046pt

(4.20)

with
G(\varphi):={\mathscr{S}}_{\varphi}(\varphi,\psi)
and
G^{j}
denoting is the composition of
G
with
itself
j
times. In particular, if
\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}(\varphi,\psi)=(\overline{G}(\varphi),A_{0}\psi)
is the averaged map (
2.18
)
and
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{n-1}
are functions on
{\mathcal{U}}\times\mathds{T}^{2}
, we have

\langle{\mathfrak{p}}\rangle^{(k)}_{[i]}(\overline{{\mathscr{S}}};\varphi)=\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\langle{\mathfrak{p}}_{i+j}\rangle(\overline{G}^{j}(\varphi)),\qquad\qquad\langle{\mathfrak{p}}\rangle^{(k)}(\overline{{\mathscr{S}}};\varphi):=\langle{\mathfrak{p}}\rangle^{(k)}_{[0]}(\overline{{\mathscr{S}}};\varphi).\vskip-5.69046pt

(4.21)

4.6
Conjugation of the averaged dynamics: proof of Lemma

2.19

Proceeding as in Subsection
3.2.2
we see that the function
\overline{h}\!:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{0}

introduced in (
2.21
) satisfies the equation

\overline{h}(\varphi)=\overline{q}(\varphi)+\overline{p}(\varphi)\,\overline{h}(\overline{G}(\varphi))
,
with
\overline{G}(\varphi)=\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}_{\varphi}(\varphi,\psi)
defined in (
2.18
) and

\displaystyle\overline{p}(\varphi)

\displaystyle:=\frac{(\overline{G}(\varphi))^{2}}{\varphi^{2}\,\overline{\mu}}=\frac{1}{1+\rho\,\partial_{\varphi}\overline{f}(0)}\left(\frac{\varphi+\rho\overline{f}(\varphi)}{\varphi}\right)^{\!\!2},

(4.22a)

\displaystyle\overline{q}(\varphi)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\overline{G}(\varphi)-\varphi\,\overline{\mu}}{\varphi^{2}\,\overline{\mu}}=\frac{\rho}{1+\rho\,\partial_{\varphi}\overline{f}(0)}\frac{\overline{f}(\varphi)-\partial_{\varphi}\overline{f}(0)\varphi}{\varphi^{2}}.

(4.22b)

Thus we can write (see (
4.21
) for the notation)

\overline{h}(\varphi)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\overline{p}^{(n)}(\varphi)\,\overline{q}({\overline{G}}^{n}(\varphi)),\qquad\qquad\overline{p}^{(n)}(\varphi)=\overline{p}^{(n)}(\overline{{\mathscr{S}}};\varphi)=\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}\overline{p}({\overline{G}}^{i}(\varphi)).\vskip-5.69046pt

(4.23)

Analogously to Lemma
3.6
the following result holds.

Lemma 4.9
.

In
{\mathcal{U}}
one has
|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|\overline{p}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,5}=1+O(\rho)
,

|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|\overline{q}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,5}=O(\rho)
and
|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|\partial_{\theta}\overline{p}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,4}=O(\rho)
.
Moreover, for any
\gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\gamma)
,
there exists
\overline{\theta}=O(1)
such that
\overline{p}(\varphi)\leq 1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime}
for
|\varphi|\leq\overline{\theta}
.

Remark 4.10
.

By reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
3.8

(and hence, actually, Lemma
2.5
),
we find that, for any
\gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\gamma)
, there exist

r>0
and
\overline{N}=O(\rho^{-1})
such that
\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}^{n}({\mathcal{U}})\subset[S_{m}-r,S_{M}+r]
and

(1+\rho\partial_{\varphi}\overline{f}){\circ}\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}^{n}\leq(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})
for all
n\geq\overline{N}
.

The following result is proved in Appendix
B.2
.

Lemma 4.11
.

Given
\gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\gamma)
and
\overline{\theta}
as in Lemma
4.9
,
consider any functions
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{n-1}
in
{\mathscr{B}}_{0,3}(\Omega,\mathds{R})
,
independent of
\psi
, such that

1.

\|{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}-1\|_{0,3}=O(\rho)
for all
i=0,\ldots,n-1
,

2.

|{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}(\varphi)|\leq 1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime}
for any

|\varphi|\leq\bar{\theta}
and for all
i=0,\ldots,n-1
,

and define
{\mathfrak{p}}^{(n)}(\varphi)={\mathfrak{p}}^{(n)}(\overline{{\mathscr{S}}};\varphi)
as in (
4.20
) with
{\mathscr{S}}=\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}
.
One has

|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\mathfrak{p}}^{(n)}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,3}\leq C(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})^{n},\qquad\qquad\|\partial_{\varphi}^{k}\overline{G}^{n}\|_{\infty}\leq C(1-{\rho}\,\gamma^{\prime})^{n}\,,\qquad k=1,2,3.

(4.24)

where the constant
C
does not depend on
n
.

Remark 4.12
.

An immediate consequence of the functions
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{n-1}
satisfying condition 1 in Lemma
4.11

is that
|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|\partial_{\varphi}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|{\hskip-0.85355pt}|_{0,2}=O(\rho)
for all
i=0,\ldots,n-1
.

Remark 4.13
.

In the following we have to consider cases in which condition 2 of Lemma
4.11
holds
for all
i=0,\ldots,n-1
except at most
n_{*}
values, for some
n_{*}<n
independent of
n
.
However, such cases are easily reduced to Lemma
4.11
. Indeed, if
{\mathfrak{p}}_{i_{1}},\ldots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{i_{n_{*}}}
are the functions
which do not satisfy condition 2, setting

I_{*}=\{i_{1},\ldots i_{n_{*}}\}

and

K_{*}=\max\{\|{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}\|_{\infty}:i\in I_{*}\}
,
we may define

\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}(\varphi)=\begin{cases}(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})^{-1}K_{*}^{-1}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}(\varphi),&i\in I_{*},\\
{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}(\varphi),&i\notin I_{*},\end{cases}\vskip-2.84544pt

so that
|\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}(\varphi)|\leq(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})^{-1}
for
i=1,\ldots,n-1
and hence, since
\|\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}-1\|_{0,3}=O(\rho)
if
\|{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}-1\|_{0,3}=O(\rho)
,
the functions
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{0},\ldots,\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{n-1}
satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma
4.11
. Therefore, we can write

{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}(\varphi)=(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})\,K_{*}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}(\varphi)
for
i\in I_{*}
and incorporate the factor
((1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})\,K_{*})^{n_{*}}

into the constant
C
in (
4.24
).

The bounds for
\overline{h}
now follow easily considering (
4.23
),
reasoning like in Subsection
3.2.3
and using the bound (
4.24
)
with
{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}=\overline{p}
for
i=0,\ldots,n-1
.
Invertibility of
\overline{{\mathscr{H}}}
follows by the same argument used in the proof of Corollary
2.12
(see Subsection
3.2.4
), and
the bounds for the function
\bar{l}
are easily obtained using the inverse function theorem.

4.7
The averaged and the continuous time system: proof of Lemma
2.21

Let
\Phi_{t}(\varphi)
be the solution of (
2.22
). Observe first that

\Phi_{\rho}(\varphi)=\varphi+\rho\overline{f}(\varphi)+\rho^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\,\partial_{\varphi}\overline{f}(\Phi_{t\rho}(\varphi))\,\overline{f}(\Phi_{t\rho}(\varphi))\,{\rm d}t\vskip-2.84544pt

(4.25)

and that for any
\gamma^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\gamma)
there exists a constant
C
independent of
\rho
such that, for all
n\geq 0
,

|\Phi_{n\rho}(\varphi)|\leq C(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime\prime})^{n},\qquad\qquad|(\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}^{n})_{\varphi}(\varphi,\psi)|\leq C(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime\prime})^{n}.\vskip-2.84544pt

(4.26)

Then, using (
2.18
) and (
4.25
), we obtain, for some
\varphi_{n}
between

(\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}^{n})_{\varphi}(\varphi,\psi)
and
\Phi_{n\rho}(\varphi)
,

\left|(\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}^{n+1})_{\varphi}(\varphi,\psi)-\Phi_{(n+1)\rho}(\varphi)\right|\leq(1+\rho\partial_{\varphi}\overline{f}(\varphi_{n}))|(\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}^{n})_{\varphi}(\varphi,\psi)-\Phi_{n\rho}(\varphi)|+C\rho^{2}(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime\prime})^{n}

where we have used (
4.26
) and the fact that
\overline{f}(0)=0
in order to
bound
|\overline{f}(\Phi_{t\rho}(\varphi))|\leq C|\Phi_{t\rho}(\varphi))|
.
Iterating we get, for suitable
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n-1}
,

|(\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}^{n})_{\varphi}(\varphi,\psi)-\Phi_{n\rho}(\varphi)|\leq C\rho^{2}\,\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime\prime})^{n-1-i}\!\!\!\prod_{j=n-i}^{n}\!\!\left(1+\rho\,\partial_{\varphi}\overline{f}(\varphi_{j})\right).\vskip-2.84544pt

Let
\overline{N}
be defined as in Remark
4.10
. By the first of (
4.26
),
there exists
\overline{M}\geq N
such that both
(\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}^{k})_{\varphi}(\varphi,\psi)
and
\Phi_{k\rho}(\varphi)

– and hence
\varphi_{k}
as well – are in
[S_{m}-r,S_{M}+r]
for
k\geq M
, with
r
such that

|1+\rho\,\partial_{\varphi}\overline{f}(\varphi)|\leq C(1-\rho\gamma^{\prime\prime})
for all
\varphi\in[S_{m}-r,S_{M}+r]
.
Thus, we get

\rho^{2}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime\prime})^{n-1-i}\!\!\prod_{j=n-i}^{n}\!\!(1+\rho\,\partial_{\varphi}\overline{f}(\varphi_{j}))\leq C\rho\left(\frac{1+\rho\|\partial_{\varphi}f\|_{\infty}}{1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime\prime}}\right)^{\overline{M}}n\rho(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime\prime})^{n},\vskip-5.69046pt

so that the follows by taking
\gamma^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\gamma)
and

\gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\gamma^{\prime\prime})
such that
n\rho(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime\prime})^{n}\leq(1-\rho\,\gamma^{\prime})^{n}
.
∎

4.8
Deviations of the conjugation: proof of Theorem
5

To compare
{\mathscr{S}}^{n}
with
\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}^{n}
, according to
(
2.29
) we need to control the deviations of
h
from
\overline{h}
and of
l
with respect to
\bar{l}
.
Since
\overline{G}(0)=0
, the map
\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}
admits the same invariant manifold

\overline{{\mathcal{W}}}:=\{(0,\psi)\,|\,\psi\in\mathds{T}^{2}\}
as
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
(see Remarks
2.17
and
3.2
).
Thus, in order to complete the program outlined at the beginning of Subsection

2.4.3
, it is more convenient to compare first
h_{1}
with
\overline{h}
and show that, in average, they are close
to each other, and then show that the same happens when comparing
h
with
h_{1}
.

The domain
\Omega_{1}
of
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}(\varphi,\psi)
and
h_{1}(\varphi,\psi)

has the form considered in Remark
2.1
, with
a_{+}(\psi)=\phi_{M}-W(\psi)

and
a_{-}(\psi)=\phi_{m}-W(\psi)
. For
\langle h_{1}(\varphi,\cdot)\rangle
to make sense we
need
h_{1}(\varphi,\psi)
to be defined for every
\psi
, but this happens only for

\varphi\in\Theta
with
\Theta
strictly contained in
{\mathcal{U}}
(see Remark

3.4
). Since we want to compare
\overline{h}(\varphi)
with

\langle h(\varphi,\cdot)\rangle
for all
\varphi\in{\mathcal{U}}
, we need to extend
h_{1}(\varphi,\psi)
to
the whole set
\Omega
. One way to accomplish this is to extend both
F
and

{\mathcal{H}}
, as described in Subsection
4.1
, to functions
F_{\rm ext}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{\rm ext}

defined on a larger domain
\Omega_{\rm ext}
such that the extended map
{\mathscr{S}}_{\rm 1,ext}(\theta,\psi)

is defined for all
(\theta,\psi)\in\Omega_{\rm ext}
. To this end we set

\Omega_{\rm ext}:={\mathcal{U}}_{\rm ext}\times\mathds{T}^{2}
,
with

{\mathcal{U}}_{\rm ext}=[\phi_{m}+\min_{\psi\in\mathds{T}^{2}}W(\psi),\phi_{M}+\max_{\psi\in\mathds{T}^{2}}W(\psi)]
,
so that we have
\Omega_{\rm ext}\supset\{(\varphi,\psi)\in\mathds{R}\times\mathds{T}^{2}:\phi_{m}+\min\{0,W(\psi)\}\leq\varphi\leq\phi_{M}+\max\{0,W(\psi)\}\}
.
Then, when considering
h(\varphi,\psi)-\overline{h}(\varphi)
, we may write, for
\varphi\in{\mathcal{U}}
,

\displaystyle h(\varphi,\psi)

\displaystyle=h_{\rm ext}(\varphi,\psi)=h_{\rm ext}(\varphi+W(\psi),\psi)-\bigl(h_{\rm ext}(\varphi+W(\psi),\psi)-h_{\rm ext}(\varphi,\psi)\bigr)

(4.27)

\displaystyle=h_{1,\rm ext}(\varphi,\psi)-W(\psi)\,\partial_{\varphi}h_{\rm ext}(\varphi,\psi)-(W(\psi))^{2}\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!{\rm d}t\,(1-t)\,\partial_{\varphi}^{2}h_{\rm ext}(\varphi-tW(\psi),\psi)

and start by studying the average of
h_{1,\rm ext}(\varphi,\psi)-\overline{h}(\varphi)
. The
next step is to show, thanks to Theorem
4
,
that the average of the other terms appearing in (
4.27
) produce corrections of order
\rho
.

Remark 4.14
.

Although we use the extended maps along the proof of Theorem
6
,
the final result does not depend on the extension we take,
because the difference between
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega

has measure of order
\rho
and hence any extended map we may consider produces
corrections which are at most of order
\rho
. A different choice could be
h_{1}(\varphi,\psi)=0
for
(\varphi,\psi)\in\Omega\setminus\Omega_{1}
;
the advantage of taking
h_{1,{\rm ext}}
as in Subsection
4.1

is that it has the same regularity of the original
h_{1}
.

Remark 4.15
.

Throughout the rest of the section, we work with the extended functions, but we
drop the subscript ‘
{\rm ext}
’ not to overwhelm the notation. Since we first
compare
\overline{h}
with
h_{1}
, to avoid confusion, we call
\theta
the first
variable not only of
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
and
h_{1}
, but also of
\overline{h}
and
\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}
.
From the above discussion, it follows that, for all

\psi\in\mathds{T}^{2}
, the range of the
variable
\theta
contains the whole interval
{\mathcal{U}}
. Only at the end, when
comparing
h
with
\bar{h}
, we compute
\overline{h}
and
h_{1}
at
\theta=\varphi
.

The rest of the subsection is mostly devoted to the proof of the following proposition and some of its implications.

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
h_{1}
and
\overline{h}
be as in (
3.12
) and in (
2.21
), respectively.
For all
\theta\in{\mathcal{U}}
, one has

\displaystyle\bigl|\left\langle h_{1}(\theta,\cdot)-\overline{h}(\theta)\right\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\rho,\qquad

\displaystyle\bigl|\left\langle(h_{1}(\theta,\cdot)-\overline{h}(\theta))^{2}\right\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\rho,

(4.28a)

\displaystyle\bigl|\left\langle\partial_{\theta}h_{1}(\theta,\cdot)-\partial_{\theta}\overline{h}(\theta)\right\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\rho,\qquad

\displaystyle\bigl|\left\langle(\partial_{\theta}h_{1}(\theta,\cdot)-\partial_{\theta}\overline{h}(\theta))^{2}\right\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\rho.

(4.28b)

After proving Proposition
4.16
, to complete the proof of Theorem
5
we need to reexpress
h_{1}

in terms of
h
. This is done in the last Subsection
4.8.5
.

4.8.1
The auxiliary map

Let
r_{2}
be such that
\theta(f(\theta,\psi)-f(0,\psi))<0
for

(\theta,\psi)\in\Lambda_{2r_{2}}
and let
\chi:{\mathcal{U}}\to\mathds{R}
be a
C^{\infty}
function
such that
\chi(\theta)=1
for
\theta\in[S_{m}-r_{2},S_{M}+r_{2}]
and
\chi(\theta)=0
for
\theta\not\in[S_{m}-2r_{2},S_{M}+2r_{2}]
. We introduce the
auxiliary map

{\mathscr{S}}_{2}(\theta,\psi):=(G_{2}(\theta,\psi),A_{0}\psi),\qquad G_{2}(\theta,\psi):=\theta+\rho f_{2}(\theta,\psi),\qquad f_{2}(\theta,\psi):=f(\theta,\psi)-\chi(\theta)f(0,\psi).

(4.29)

Remark 4.17
.

Instead of
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
, one might like to consider the simpler map

{\mathscr{S}}_{3}(\theta,\psi):=(G_{3}(\theta,\psi),A_{0}\psi)
, with
G_{3}(\theta,\psi)\!:=\!\theta+\rho f_{3}(\theta,\psi)
and
f_{3}(\theta,\psi)\!:=\!f(\theta,\psi)-f(0,\psi)
.
However, it could happen that
f_{3}(\theta,\psi)\!=\!0
also for some
\theta\not=0
,
so that
{\mathscr{S}}_{3}
would not satisfy Hypothesis
2
and
\overline{{\mathcal{W}}}

might fail to be a global attractor for
(\Omega,{\mathscr{S}}_{3})
; hence, in general,
{\mathscr{S}}_{3}
could not be conjugated with
{\mathscr{S}}_{0}
.
On the other hand, if one is willing to restrict the map
{\mathscr{S}}

to a smaller set inside
\Omega
, say the set
\Lambda_{2r_{2}}
defined above, then one can define

f_{2}(\theta,\psi)=f(\theta,\psi)-f(0,\psi)
, without introducing the function
\chi
,
and the corresponding map
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
satisfies all Hypotheses
1
–
3
.
The same goal might be achieved by assuming stronger hypotheses on the map
{\mathscr{S}}
,
say by requiring it to be uniformly contracting along the direction of the slow variable on the whole
\Omega
,
but the conditions so introduced would be too restrictive and unnecessary for the result to hold.

If we set
F_{1}(\theta,\psi)=\rho f_{1}(\theta,\psi)
in (
3.9
), with

f_{1}(\theta,\psi):=f(\theta+W(\psi),\psi)-f(W(\psi),\psi)
, we can write
f_{1}(\theta)=f_{2}(\theta,\psi)+\zeta(\theta,\psi)
, with

\displaystyle\zeta(\theta,\psi)

\displaystyle:=c_{0}(\theta)\,{\mathfrak{f}}(\psi)+c_{1}(\theta,\psi)\,W(\psi)+c_{2}(\theta,\psi)\,W(\psi)^{2},

(4.30a)

\displaystyle c_{0}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=\chi(\theta)-1,\phantom{\int_{0}{\rm d}t}

(4.30b)

\displaystyle c_{1}(\theta,\psi)

\displaystyle:=\left(\partial_{\theta}f(\theta,\psi)-\chi(\theta)\,\partial_{\theta}f(0,\psi)\right),

(4.30c)

\displaystyle c_{2}(\theta,\psi)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{1}{\rm d}t\,(1-t)\left(\partial_{\theta}^{2}f(\theta+tW(\psi),\psi)-\chi(\theta)\,\partial_{\theta}^{2}f(tW(\psi),\psi)\right),

(4.30d)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}(\psi)

\displaystyle:=f(W(\psi),\psi)=\rho^{-1}(W(A_{0}\psi)-W(\psi)).\phantom{\sum_{i}}

(4.30e)

where the identity (
4.30e
) derives from (
3.1
), with
F=\rho f
, and is used at length in the following.

Remark 4.18
.

The definition of the function
\chi
ensures that
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
satisfies Hypotheses
1
–
3
,
provided
\rho
is such that

\rho\,\partial_{\theta}f_{2}(\theta,\psi)>-1
for all
(\theta,\psi)\in\Omega
.
Thus, if we wish to use the results in Subsections
2.3
and

3.2
with
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
in place of
{\mathscr{S}}
we need to
restrict
\rho
to an interval
(0,\rho_{0})
with
\rho_{0}>0
possibly smaller
than
\rho_{*}
,
as defined in Remark
2.13
. This is not a problem since we are mainly
interested in the regime in which
\rho
tends to zero. Moreover, for any
fixed
\rho_{0}\in(0,\rho_{*})
, the bounds in Theorem
5
become
trivial for
\rho\in[\rho_{0},\rho_{*})
by taking, if needed, larger values for the
involved constants
C
(see also Remark
4.20
).

Remark 4.19
.

By construction
\langle f_{2}(\theta,\cdot)\rangle=\langle f(\theta,\cdot)\rangle
for
\theta\in{\mathcal{U}}
and
\partial_{\theta}f_{2}(0,\psi)=\partial_{\theta}f(0,\psi)
. On the other hand, the fact that
f_{1}(\theta)=f_{2}(\theta,\psi)+\zeta(\theta,\psi)

shows
that
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
can be seen as a regularization of
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
. In particular, one has

{\mathscr{S}}_{2}(0,\psi)=(0,A_{0}\psi)
, so that also the invariant manifold of
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
is
given by
\overline{{\mathcal{W}}}=\{(0,\psi):\psi\in\mathds{T}^{2}\}
, and it is thus the
same as that of
\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}
and
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
(see Remarks
2.17
and
3.2
).

From Theorem
2
, with
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
instead of
{\mathscr{S}}
, and Remark
4.19

it follows that there exists a set
\Omega_{2}\subset\mathds{R}\times\mathds{T}^{2}
and a
map
{\mathscr{H}}_{2}\!:\Omega\to\Omega_{2}
of the
form

{\mathscr{H}}_{2}(\theta,\psi):=({\mathcal{H}}_{2}(\theta,\psi),\psi),\qquad{\mathcal{H}}_{2}(\theta,\psi):=\theta+\theta^{2}h_{2}(\theta,\psi),\vskip-2.84544pt

(4.31)

which conjugates
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
to its linearization

(\mu(\psi)\theta,A_{0}\psi)
, i.e. such that

\mu(\psi){\mathcal{H}}_{2}(\theta,\psi)={\mathcal{H}}_{2}({\mathscr{S}}_{2}(\theta,\psi))
,
with
\mu(\psi)
defined after (
4.8
).
Introducing the functions

\displaystyle p_{2}(\theta,\psi)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{1+\rho\partial_{\theta}f_{2}(0,\psi)}\left(\frac{\theta+\rho f_{2}(\theta,\psi)}{\theta}\right)^{2},

(4.32a)

\displaystyle q_{2}(\theta,\psi)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\rho}{1+\rho\,\partial_{\theta}f_{2}(0,\psi)}\frac{f_{2}(\theta,\psi)-\partial_{\theta}f_{2}(0,\psi)\theta}{\theta^{2}},

(4.32b)

we get

h_{2}(\theta,\psi):=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}p^{(n)}_{2}(\theta,\psi)\,q_{2}({\mathscr{S}}_{2}^{n}(\theta,\psi)),\qquad p^{(n)}_{2}(\theta,\psi):=\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}p_{2}({\mathscr{S}}_{2}^{i}(\theta,\psi)).\vskip-5.69046pt

(4.33)

In order to study the average of
h_{1}(\theta,\psi)-\overline{h}(\theta)
and of its derivative, we split

\displaystyle h_{1}(\theta,\psi)-\overline{h}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\left(h_{1}(\theta,\psi)-h_{2}(\theta,\psi)\right)+\left(h_{2}(\theta,\psi)-\overline{h}(\theta)\right),

(4.34a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta}h_{1}(\theta,\psi)-\partial_{\theta}\overline{h}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\left(\partial_{\theta}h_{1}(\theta,\psi)-\partial_{\theta}h_{2}(\theta,\psi)\right)+\left(\partial_{\theta}h_{2}(\theta,\psi)-\partial_{\theta}\overline{h}(\theta)\right),

(4.34b)

and study separately the two contributions in each of (
4.34a
) and (
4.34b
). This is the content of
Propositions
4.26
,
4.27
,
4.42
, and
4.43
below,
which combined immediately imply Proposition
4.16
.
For both maps
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
and
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
, the
\theta
-component vanishes at
\theta=0
.
However, while
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
depends on
W
and hence inherits the low regularity of the
invariant manifold,
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
has the same regularity as
{\mathscr{S}}
.
Therefore, through the splitting (
4.34
), we control first the deviations of
h_{2}
from
\overline{h}

(see Subsections
4.8.2
and
4.8.3
) using the regularity of
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
and the
fact that
{\mathscr{S}}
and
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
share the same averaged map
\overline{{\mathscr{S}}}
; next we
show that the deviations of
h_{1}
from
h_{2}
are small thanks to (
4.30a
) and Theorem
4

(see Subsection
4.8.4
).

Remark 4.20
.

For
{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
to satisfy Hypotheses
1
–
3
we need to restrict
the maximum value allowed for
\rho
to
\rho_{0}
since the condition
